- **W20** `Effect.timeout` that forks a fiber per call: `timeout.After(d) { … }`, an ordinary function whose row adds `Expired` ([runtime.md, Timeouts](runtime.md#timeouts)).

The table has one row per std package, the prelude included. The Effect module is a file of `packages/effect/src` at pin `460272d3`, the pin of `conformance/effect-cases.json`. The last column is the package's lowering against hand-written Go: a benchmark the repository declares, as `<package dir>.Benchmark<Name>`, with its last recorded result and where the result is kept; or `pending:` and the file that holds the command; or `none:` and the reason; or `counted:` and a link to the std count receipt, [std-count](../receipts/std-count.md), which `go run ./scripts/stdcount` writes: user-space instructions and allocations per operation of each hot function's Ergo form against the Go function, and against hand Go where the receipt names one, from one Ergo and one Go program whose results agree (std-better SB0). Counts hold under host load, as wall times do not. A result is a measurement on a shared host unless the cell says otherwise; a speed claim needs a quiet-host run (common rule). `TestStdStrengthsTable` finds the packages from `std.Packages()` and the prelude, never from a written list, and fails for a package with no row, a row for no package, an empty cell, a workaround id outside W1 to W20, an Ergo construct with no spec link, and a benchmark that the named package's tests do not declare; it fails when the count receipt is missing, when a `counted:` cell does not link it or names a package the receipt does not count, and when the receipt counts a package whose cell is not `counted:`. It checks names, not truth: a reviewer reads each row against the package source.

| `utf8` | `String.ts` | W2, W3, W10 | data-first pure functions; Go's two results of `DecodeRuneInString` as the record `Decoded`, where Effect's `String` has no rune decoder and JavaScript's `codePointAt` gives `undefined` past the end ([strings and strconv](#strings-and-strconv) 8; [Pure bodies](#pure-bodies)) | counted: [std-count](../receipts/std-count.md), instructions and allocations per operation of the Ergo form against Go's function: `RuneCount` equal; `DecodeRuneInString` 1.57x (36 against 23 instructions: the `Decoded` record) |
| `tz` | `DateTime.ts` (the zoned half: `Zoned`, `makeZoned` with `disambiguation`, `zoneMakeNamed`, `zoneMakeOffset`), `CurrentTimeZone` | W4, W5, W6, W9 | the closed union `Resolved = Unique \| Gap \| Overlap` matched by the caller, where `disambiguation` is a string option with a default; `Compatible` the one named choice; `Zone` with construction authority (`FromTZif … fails BadData`, `FixedOffset … fails BadOffset`); the database is the operation type `Zones` in the row, where Effect provides a `CurrentTimeZone` layer ([tz](#tz)) | `internal/sketch/lower.BenchmarkStdTz`: `OffsetAt`, `Compatible(Resolve)`, `FromTZif` of America/Toronto, against Go's `t.In(loc).Zone()`, `time.Date` in `loc` and `LoadLocationFromTZData`; quiet run pending in `wf10/std-spike/bench-pending.md` |
| `tz/iana` | `DateTime.ts` (`zoneMakeNamed`, which reads the host's `Intl` database) | W4 | std's own IANA copy as a `Zones` provider value, chosen in the program's context; names are the database's ([tz](#tz) 2) | none: a provider over embedded bytes; its `Load` is `FromTZif`, measured in `internal/sketch/lower.BenchmarkStdTz` |
| `tz/host` | `DateTime.ts` (`zoneMakeNamed` over the host) | W4, W9 | the host's zone files as a `Zones` provider that only `New() needs Ambient` makes ([tz](#tz) 2) | none: one host file read, then `FromTZif` |
| `codec` | `Schema.ts` (`encode`, `decode`, `ParseResult` issues) | W5, W6, W7, W9 | no Schema value: the codec is derived from the declared type by the predeclared `encode(v, w)` and `decode[T](r)` over the prelude's `Writer` and `Reader` events ([types.md, Codecs](types.md#codecs)); decode's row is the union of what the type reaches (`WireWrongKind`, `WireMissingField`, `WireUnknownTag`, `Unsatisfied`, each `ParseX`'s row); `Events` and `Replay` record and replay the events, and its laws hold the round trip | none: the events are a test's record; the format-specialised path is std json's (its row) |
| `toml` | `ConfigProvider.ts` (external typed configuration; no TOML parser in Effect core) | W4, W5, W6 | declared records and the shared codec derivation, typed `Malformed` and `wired[T]` rows, not Config/Schema builders ([toml](#toml)) | `runtime/v1.BenchmarkTOMLParse`: parse-only baseline; no measured parity. Hand-Go control: the same parser followed by encoding/json into an equivalent Go config record; generated decoder/control comparison pending in `wf10/cli-units/cu7/bench-pending.md`. Current tree plus JSON transcode costs an extra pass; direct Reader events are the follow-up. |
| `json` | `Schema.ts` (`parseJson`, `encode`, `decode`), `JSON.parse`/`JSON.stringify` | W5, W6, W7, W9 | `Codec[T wire]{MaxBytes, MaxDepth}` over the derived codec ([types.md, Codecs](types.md#codecs)) with `Encode` (cannot fail) and `Decode` (`fails Malformed \| wired[T]`: phase 1 checks the whole text, phase 2 is the derived `decode[T]` with each failure's path); `Text` is a `Writer`, `Reader` a `Reader`; the law `FastAgreesWithWriter` holds the compiler's fast path to the Writer path | `internal/sketch/lower.BenchmarkJSON` (a 1 KB deploy record against `encoding/json` and `encoding/json/v2` over hand structs with the same keys): encode calls the Text's methods directly, no interface call per event (`lower/wiredirect.go`), 7 allocations to Go's 8; decode is the Reader interface path, 97 allocations to Go's 30 (an `Env` per adapter call); ratios bench-pending on a quiet host (S-json bench-pending.md) |
| `jsonschema` | `JSONSchema.ts` (`make`: a JSON Schema written from a `Schema`; Effect checks values by the `Schema`, not by the JSON Schema) | W5, W6 | `Schema` with an unexported field, made only by the checked `Of` and `Parse`, so a document whose keywords have the wrong kinds or whose `$ref` leaves it is refused once, not at each check; `Invalid` as a typed failure with its JSON pointer ([jsonschema](#jsonschema)) | pending: JS0's receipt counts one `Conforms` of a decoded value against a hand Go walk of the same schema (`go run ./scripts/opcount -control -rounds 5 benchmarks/jsonschema/conforms`) |
| `xml` | none: Effect has no XML codec; Go's `encoding/xml` is the control | W5, W6, W7, W9 | `Codec[T wire]{Root, MaxBytes, MaxDepth}` over the derived codec, as std json's, with `Encode` (cannot fail) and `Decode` (`fails Malformed \| wired[T]`: the whole text is checked first, then read with each failure's path); the record's field type `Attribute` says what is an attribute, where Go uses a struct tag ([xml](#xml)) | none: no Go benchmark; `examples/catalog receipt` measures it: encode 2 allocations per catalog, through `Text`'s one provider called directly and its tags joined by the law `Text.RawJoins`; decode through the sealed `Cursor` |
| `mcp` | `@effect/ai` `McpServer` (`toolkit`, `layerStdio`) | W5, W6, W7 | tools as plain values in a `Server` and the transport as an operation type with its law, bound in `main`, where Effect registers tools into a `Layer`; the protocol's two failures as `Refused` (JSON-RPC error) and a `ToolError` result, never mixed ([mcp](#mcp) 2) | none: a protocol adapter; its cost is std `json` and `jsonschema`, counted there |
| `mcp/memory` | `@effect/ai` `McpServer` over a test layer | W4 | a session as a value: the client's messages given at the start and `Sent` read after ([mcp](#mcp)) | none: an in-memory provider |
| `mcp/stdio` | `@effect/ai` `McpServer.layerStdio` | W4, W9 | standard input and output as a provider only `New() needs Ambient` makes ([mcp](#mcp) 3) | none: std `io`'s `Lines` and `Println` |
| `uuid` | none: Effect has no uuid | W4, W14 | a `Generator`'s V7 ids are ordered within a millisecond too (law `V7IsOrderedInProcess`); `V4` and `V7` draw from `rand.Secure` and read `time.Clock` in the caller's row, so a test binds `rand.NewSeededSecure` and `time.NewManual` and gets the same ids every run; a V7 sorts by its millisecond (law `V7IsOrderedByTime`); `Parse` fails with a typed `Malformed` ([keyed digests](#keyed-digests)) | `runtime/v1/digest.BenchmarkDigest`: `RandSecureBytes` against `crypto/rand.Read` |
| `semver` | none: Effect has no version module; `Bun.semver` and Go's `golang.org/x/mod/semver` are the controls | W3, W6, W14 | `Version` and `Range` records, the comparator `Op` as a union matched exhaustively, `Malformed` in the row of `Parse` and `ParseRange`, laws for the order ([semver](#semver); [types.md, Unions](types.md#unions); [rows.md, Catch](rows.md#catch)) | pending: per-call instructions of `Parse` and `Compare` against x/mod/semver and the Go floor are in SQ4's receipt (`std-priv/SQ4/receipt-1.tsv`); std-count has no semver row yet |

*Prior art:* ReScript makes pipe-first `->` a plain call and uncurries by default (W2, W3); Elixir's `|>` is a macro and `with` chains the happy path (W1, W2), and `@derive` generates protocol code (W7, W8); Gleam's `use` replaces callbacks with plain lines and its `Result` replaces exceptions (W1, W5), with no type classes (W16); Roc and MoonBit derive equality, hashing and codecs (W7, W8), and Roc gives each host effect as a platform capability (W4, W18); Elm has no derivation and writes decoders by hand, the shape W7 and W17 avoid; Koka's evidence passing makes a tail-resumptive handler a direct call, as Ergo's environment of capabilities does (W14).

## Std is ordinary

The standard library is an ordinary set of packages ([NORTH_STAR.md](../../NORTH_STAR.md#owner-rules); [decision](../decisions.md#owner-direction-2026-11-12)). Anything a std package can do, any module can do through the same written mechanism: a declaration in its source that the compiler reads. No checker rule, lowering, optimizer, cache or tool decides by a std name or by std membership, with two exceptions, both written here:

1. **The seam.** The compiler may know that std exists to find it, ship it, cache it and name it. These files hold that knowledge, and they change no meaning:
   - `internal/sketch/check/check.go`: loads the embedded prelude and std packages and marks them std.
   - `internal/sketch/check/decl.go`: a std import path always names std, so no package shadows it.
   - `internal/sketch/check/snapshot/snapshot.go`: the checked std snapshot.
   - `internal/sketch/check/info.go`: the accessor of the std mark.
   - `internal/sketchfront/load.go`: std paths resolve to the embedded std, and a module path does not take a std package's name. The shipped test plan declares the prelude a required native-only root; a root with that property accounts for missing native verdicts and fails on a native invocation error regardless of its name.
   - `internal/sketchfront/sketchfront.go`: the digest of std's sources is part of every build key.
   - `internal/sketch/lower/lower.go`, `internal/sketch/lower/naming.go` and `internal/sketch/lower/sources.go`: a std package ships in the compiler, so the lowering names its declarations and positions from the embedded files, not from a unit, and its law ledger ships beside them. Which ledger answers for a provider's laws is chosen by the module that declares it, in one function of the lowering (`evidenceFor`): the build's module's `ergo.laws` for its packages, the shipped ledger for the shipped ones.
   - `internal/sketch/check/factguard.go`: the guard lines of the reviewed tables in `runtime/v1` (`v1.GoSyncCallbacks`, `v1.PureImports`) ship in that same ledger, because the tables and their guards ship with the compiler; the checker reads them from it (`FactLedger`; interop.md, Foreign facts 7).
2. **The predeclared names.** The language gives a meaning to some names that the prelude declares, and to the operation names of its protocols. These functions read such a name:

| Reader | Predeclared name |
| --- | --- |
| `internal/rpcgen/rpcgen.go:importName` | a prelude name, written unqualified |
| `internal/rpcgen/rpcgen.go:pairOf` | `Wire`, the codec's conversion protocol |
| `internal/sketch/check/authority.go:authorityText` | a prelude constructor, named unqualified |
| `internal/sketch/check/authority.go:isAmbient` | `Ambient` |
| `internal/sketch/check/authority.go:isTestAmbient` | `TestAmbient` |
| `internal/sketch/check/call.go:checker.preludeOp` | a prelude operation, reached by the dot form |
| `internal/sketch/check/call.go:isUnreachable` | `Unreachable` |
| `internal/sketch/check/contextneeds.go:checker.readContextHeader` | `TestAmbient` in a named context's needs row: the test runner gives it; no with site does ([rows](rows.md#context)) |
| `internal/sketch/check/contextvalues.go:contextRow` | `Context` |
| `internal/sketch/check/durable.go:pureSeer.step` | `Machine`, a machine literal whose Step a pure parameter sees |
| `internal/sketch/check/durable.go:pureSeer.stepValue` | `Machine`'s `Step` |
| `internal/sketch/check/entry.go:checker.checkErrorMethods` | `Error`, the method of a failure |
| `internal/sketch/check/extern.go:IsGoError` | `go.Error` |
| `internal/sketch/check/extern.go:predeclaredExtern` | the prelude's externs, intrinsics whose bodies the lowering writes |
| `internal/sketch/check/implements.go:checker.implementsClause` | a prelude type, hidden by a package's own type of its name |
| `internal/sketch/check/implements.go:checker.preludeContract` | `Closer`, `Keeping` |
| `internal/sketch/check/implements.go:checker.recordLegacyReleases` | `Closer` |
| `internal/sketch/check/implements.go:isGoCloser` | `io.Closer` |
| `internal/sketch/check/laws.go:lawClosures` | the prelude's providers in a law |
| `internal/sketch/check/lintfacts.go:IsConsole` | `Console` |
| `internal/sketch/check/lintfacts.go:IsScheduler` | `Scheduler`, and its `Sleep` |
| `internal/sketch/check/loops.go:checker.preludeOrigin` | `Range`, `Seq` |
| `internal/sketch/check/match.go:reusableBinderName` | `Decision` |
| `internal/sketch/check/ownership.go:checker.checkOwnership` | `Close` |
| `internal/sketch/check/releaseneeds.go:checker.releaseWider` | `Close` |
| `internal/sketch/check/stmt.go:checker.isErrorValue` | `go.Error` |
| `internal/sketch/derive/eval.go:evaluator.callee` | `Of`, the derive plan's constructor |
| `internal/sketch/eval/machine.go:interp.machineStep` | `Enter`, `Stay`, `Emit`, `Tell`, `Done`, `Next` |
| `internal/sketch/lower/calls.go:funcCtx.callee` | a prelude function, written unqualified |
| `internal/sketch/lower/decls.go:isPreludeType` | `Option`, `Some`, `None` |
| `internal/sketch/lower/decls.go:lowerer.intrinsic` | `Append`, `Delete`, `Put`, `Take` |
| `internal/sketch/lower/getmatch.go:getPattern` | `Some`'s `Value`, the comma-ok form of `Get` |
| `internal/sketch/lower/gocall.go:isOptionType` | `Option` |
| `internal/sketch/lower/library.go:hostProvides` | `Console`, `Ambient` |
| `internal/sketch/lower/module.go:lowerer.hostFields` | `Console`, `Ambient` |
| `internal/tsproj/project.go:emitter.declare` | `Wire` |
| `internal/tsproj/project.go:emitter.schema` | `Option` |
| `internal/sketch/check/entry.go:isPreludeMachine` | `Machine`, by declaration |
| `internal/sketch/check/entry.go:isPreludeMachineValue` | `Machine` and `InputMachine`, by declaration |
| `internal/sketch/check/expr.go:checker.compositeLit` | the Map's provider form `hashTable`, which the prelude writes as its record |
| `internal/sketch/check/laws.go:Law.Title` | a prelude law, named unqualified |
| `internal/sketch/check/mapprovider.go:providerParts` | `hashTable`, the Map's provider form ([types](types.md#maps), Maps 5 and 6) |
| `internal/sketch/check/mapprovider.go:checker.providerEqual` | `==` of provider Maps is the table's `Equal`; the prelude compares its records as records ([types](types.md#maps), Maps 3) |
| `internal/sketch/check/mapshape.go:checker.derivedNormal` | `NormalForm`, the operation of `Hashing` the checker derives ([types](types.md#maps), Maps 6) |
| `internal/sketch/check/mapshape.go:checker.writeShapes` | `Hashing`'s `NormalForm` and `NormalKey`; the prelude's own providers write theirs ([types](types.md#maps), Maps 6) |
| `internal/sketch/check/mapprovider.go:checker.isStructural` | `Structural`, the Map's default provider |
| `internal/sketch/check/mapprovider.go:checker.preludeTypeObj` | the prelude's Map types, from its scope in its own check |
| `internal/sketch/eval/cases.go:qualifiedLaw` | a prelude law, named unqualified |

The reviewed facts of a Go body (pure, host, inert, its hidden arguments, moves only, a moving callback and its keeper, a release that stops running code, an operation that drains lends) are read in one place, `check.BodyOf` and `check.OpBodyOf`, by the body the extern's declaration names. An extern of any package that binds the same body gets the same facts; the declaring package is never read (`TestBodyFactsFollowTheBody`). Whether a package may bind the runtime at all is the extern rule's question, not the facts'.

Every other read of std membership or a std name is debt, listed with the change that removes it in `internal/residue/testdata/std-privileges.txt`; the list only shrinks. `TestCompilerReadsNoStdMembership` holds the source to these lists. The proof is the copy: a std package copied into a user module checks, lowers and counts the same (`TestStdCopyAgrees`: every std package's copy is the same).

## The body contract

An `extern go` declaration binds a Go body. The **body contract** is everything the compiler may rely on about that body ([GLOSSARY](../../GLOSSARY.md), Body contract). It is written for any module, and std reads it the same way (STD-NOPRIV NP4a; decisions Q2 and Q6 of the plan). Each fact has one home:

- **A clause on the Ergo declaration**, when a caller's check depends on the fact. The checker reads Ergo source, and a caller sees only the declaration.
- **A mark on the Go body**, a directive line in the doc comment of the Go function, when only the lowering needs the fact, or when the fact is a property of the Go code that the reviewed rules check against that code.

The compiler reads every fact by the body that the declaration names, never by the package that declares it. The checker reads them in `check.BodyOf` and `check.OpBodyOf`, and the lowering in `lower/bodycontract.go`.

| Fact | Meaning | Home | Read by | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| pure | A call runs no capability, starts no task and calls no unknown code. | Mark `//ergo:pure`, checked against the body (`pureRefusal`: imports, host reach). | the checker's footprint, the lowering's purity (Pure bodies) | built (NP4b) |
| host | The body reaches host state. | Mark `//ergo:host`. A body that reaches the host without it is refused (`hostReach`). | the checker | built |
| moves only | A generic body only moves its type parameters' values. | runtime/v1's table `movesOnly` (`v1.MovesOnly`), keyed by the body. | the checker | built; the mark is a follow-up |
| keeps a callback | The body runs a callback on a child task, whose captures move to its root, and a parameter or the result keeps it. | runtime/v1's tables (`v1.MovingBody`, `v1.CallbackKeeper`). | the checker | built; the mark is a follow-up |
| drains | An operation does not return while its keeper's callback still runs. | runtime/v1's table (`v1.DrainsLends`). | the checker | built |
| stops running code | A release that stops code still running. | runtime/v1's table (`v1.StopsRunning`). | the checker | built |
| makes its result | Every `return` gives `make(…)` or `nil`, so the result is fresh. | The body's form, read from the Go code (`lower/gobody.go`). | the lowering | built |
| appends | The result is the first (slice) operand with elements added at its end, as Go's `append` gives, so the value rule treats the call as the prelude's `Append`. | The body's form: every `return` gives the first parameter or a call whose first argument is it (`lower/gobody.go`, `goBodyAppends`). | the lowering | built |
| takes its operand over | An owned form takes the first argument over, so a consumed argument's storage is kept with no copy. | Mark `//ergo:owned Name` naming a body of the same shape. | the lowering | built |
| waits | Which task a call waits on, or none. | Mark `//ergo:wait KIND` (Calling policy, above). | the lowering | built (NP4d) |
| releases | The body is a checked `Close`'s or an exit action's release. | Mark `//ergo:release caller` or `frame`. | the lowering | built (NP4d) |
| reads context | The body reads the calling task's context values. | Its hidden `ContextPackage` parameter. | the lowering | built |
| keeps as written | A pure identity of one parameter: a rewrite over functions never takes a call tree in its argument as its root or a part of its match (types.md, Laws 14). | The line `// ergo:aswritten` above an Ergo function, checked against its shape (`check.AsWritten`); no runtime/v1 body states it. | the checker and the lowering (`lower/ir_rewrite.go`) | built (LF6c; an Ergo line since AW-ERGO, any package) |
| hidden arguments | Values the lowering supplies at the call. | The body's Go parameter types (Hidden arguments, above). | both | built |
| requires a pure function | A parameter whose argument must be pure (a durable step). | Clause `pure` on the parameter, in the family of `escaping`, `takes` and `lent` (Q6). | the checker | NP3a |
| a Go type for an Ergo type | A record, operation type or defined basic type (a refinement too) that is a runtime Go type. | `extern go` on the type declaration names its Go type (Q6), checked against it (Types 3, 4 and 6). | the checker and the lowering | built (NP4c) |

The tables of runtime/v1 (`moves.go`) and the `inert` fact of `actor.NewHost` (`v1.Inert`) are keyed by the body's name in runtime/v1, never by an Ergo name, so a copy of a std package binds the same facts (`TestBodyFactsFollowTheBody`). They become marks when another module needs them. A module's own body (interop.md, Trusted Go 3) has none of them yet.

**The hidden-argument convention is the runtime's public Go calling convention.** The types `*v1.Scope`, `v1.Site`, `*rt.Task`, `v1.Sleeper`, `v1.ContextPackage` and `v1.Releaser[T]`, in the order of Hidden arguments, and the marks of the table above are a public interface of `ergo.local/prototype/runtime`. They change only with the language version, as Go's own exported API does. A Go body in any module can follow the convention.

**The trust rule (Q2).** Go that Ergo does not check is bound only when someone vouches for it. The root module's `ergo.mod` names the modules whose Go it trusts:

```
module app
trust go app
```

The toolchain vouches for what it ships, std and the prelude, by the same rule, with no line. An untrusted package's extern of the runtime, or of a body of its module's own Go package, is S108 naming the line to write ([interop.md, Trusted Go](interop.md#trusted-go)).

Two examples follow. A trusted module binds a runtime body with hidden arguments as std does:

```
extern go ergo.local/prototype/runtime/v1.ContextBound func contextBound(key string) bool
```

and binds a body of its own Go package:

```go
//ergo:wait caller
func Poll(t *rt.Task, n int) int { t.Check(); return n + 1 }
```

```
extern go example.com/app/gohost.Poll func poll(n int) int
```

Decided: a mark is a directive line, `//ergo:NAME ARGS`, in the doc comment of the Go function, one per fact, each at most once (Go's own `//go:` directive form). Decided by "Go idioms beat novel notation".
Decided: a body with no `//ergo:wait` is refused, not read as `none`. A missing mark is a missing fact, and failing closed is the rule for unchecked code.
Decided: the hidden arguments are read from the Go parameter types, with no mark, because the Go type is already the binding and a second spelling could disagree.
Decided: a type names its Go type with the binding form `extern go PATH.Name type …`, whose path is `ergo.local/prototype/runtime/v1`. A generated binding's host type uses the same form with a Go package's path (interop.md, Binding files); the path tells them apart, and a runtime path is held to the runtime's type, never read as a host type.
Decided: the four tables of `runtime/v1/moves.go` stay tables keyed by the body until a second module needs those facts. The key is the body, so they grant std no privilege.

*Prior art:* OCaml's `external` attributes (`[@@noalloc]`, `[@unboxed]`) sit on the declaration and are read for any module. GHC's primop attributes sit on each primop's declaration. Go's compiler reads the runtime's ABI from the runtime's declarations (`mkbuiltin.go`). Erlang NIFs state their scheduling class (dirty CPU or IO) on the C function. Gleam's and ReScript's externals have no hidden arguments and no facts: the call is opaque.

## log

Std `log` (std-spike C3) writes log entries through the `Sink` in the context, with the annotations in force ([Context values: trace and log](#v1-packages-task-scope-queue-and-slice)). It follows Effect's `Logger` and `LogLevel` (a logger is a service, annotations are fiber-local, a minimum level filters) and Go's `log/slog` (levels and their numbers, `JSONHandler`'s lines).

| Name | Signature |
| --- | --- |
| `Level` | `LevelDebug \| LevelInfo \| LevelWarn \| LevelError`; `Rank(l Level) int`, `Name(l Level) string`, `Admits(least Level, l Level) bool` |
| `Entry`, `Attr` | `Entry { Level Level, Message string, Attrs []Attr }`; `Attr { Key string, Text string }` |
| `Sink` | `Sink { Enabled(l Level) bool, Write(e Entry) }` with the law `EnabledIsMonotone` |
| logging | `Log(level Level, message string, attrs []Attr) needs Sink`; `Debug`, `Info`, `Warn`, `Error(message string) needs Sink`; `Annotations() []Attr` |
| providers | `NewJSONLines(out io.Stderr, clock time.Clock, least Level) JSONLines`; `NewRecorder(least Level) Recorder`, `Entries(r Recorder) []Entry` |

1. **Levels.** A level is a union, so a match covers each one. `Rank` is slog's number (Debug -4, Info 0, Warn 4, Error 8) and `Name` its text (`DEBUG`, `INFO`, `WARN`, `ERROR`).
2. **Filtering.** A sink is made with a least level and writes the entries whose level ranks at or above it (`Admits`); a sink that writes a level writes every level above it (law `Sink.EnabledIsMonotone`). `Log` asks `Sink.Enabled` before it builds an entry, so a level the sink drops costs one call and reads no annotation (law `LevelFilters`).
3. **Annotations.** An entry's attributes are the annotations in force for the running task, outermost first, an inner annotation of a key in the place of the outer one, then the call's own `attrs` (law `AnnotationsNest`). `Annotations` reads them through the `runtime/v1` body `ContextPairs` (context.go), the context values of the reading package; a task sees the annotations in force when it started.
4. **Providers.** `JSONLines` writes each entry as one line on its `io.Stderr`: `{"time":…,"level":…,"msg":…}` and then each attribute as a member, the time the clock's reading in RFC 3339 with nanoseconds, all as slog's `JSONHandler` writes them (`TestStdLogMatchesSlog`, against Go's handler on seeded entries). Its strings are json's: a byte of invalid UTF-8 is json's surrogate escape, which reads back, where slog writes U+FFFD. `Recorder` keeps the entries for a test; its copies share them.
5. **Not taken from Effect**, each a workaround (W4, W12): `Logger.make` over an `Effect` callback and `Logger.zip`/`batched` combinators (a provider is an ordinary record that implements `Sink`, and one that writes to two sinks holds both); `Effect.annotateLogs` as a combinator (`with log.Annotate` is the block form); spans in log lines (`trace`). Not taken from slog: attribute values other than text, groups, `With` handlers.
6. **Laws and runners.** The laws are beside their declarations in `sink.ergo`. Lower `TestStdLogLawsRun` restates them as Ergo tests, `Sink.EnabledIsMonotone` over both providers at each least level.

*Prior art:* Effect's `Logger`, `LogLevel` and `Effect.annotateLogs`; Go's `log/slog` (`Level`, `Handler.Enabled`, `JSONHandler`). Elixir's `Logger` keeps metadata per process (`Logger.metadata`) and filters by a level before it formats, as `Log` does; Gleam has no logger in its standard library (`gleam_erlang` binds Erlang's `logger`), and ReScript binds `console`. Each compiles a call below the level to a check and nothing else, as `Enabled` is here.

## stream

Std `stream` (std-spike C4) holds the `Seq` operators that read time: Effect `Stream`'s `throttle`, `debounce` and `groupedWithin`, Rx's `throttleFirst` and `debounceTime`. They are Ergo over `time`, with no Go body of their own.

| Name | Signature |
| --- | --- |
| `Throttle` | `Throttle[T](s Seq[T], d duration, clock time.Clock) Seq[T]` |
| `Debounce` | `Debounce[T](s Seq[T], d duration, clock time.Clock) Seq[T]` |
| `GroupedWithin` | `GroupedWithin[T](s Seq[T], n int, d duration, clock time.Clock) Seq[[]T]` |
| `Debounced` | `Debounced[T](src queue.Queue[T], d duration, out queue.Queue[T]) fails queue.Closed needs Scheduler \| time.Clock` |

1. **Throttle** keeps the first value and each later value that arrives `d` or more after the last value it kept; it drops the rest (law `ThrottleMatchesModel`). This is Effect's `throttle` with one unit per `d` and the `enforce` strategy.
2. **Debounce** keeps each value whose next value arrives `d` or more after it, and the last value: a burst of values less than `d` apart leaves only its last (law `DebounceMatchesModel`). A kept value leaves when the next value arrives or the source ends. That is the first moment a `Seq` can tell that `d` of quiet has passed.
3. **GroupedWithin** gives the values in chunks, in order. A chunk starts at its first value. It closes when it holds `n` values, or when a value arrives `d` or more after the chunk's first value; that value starts the next chunk. No chunk is empty, and the chunks joined are the source (laws `GroupedWithinMatchesModel`, `GroupedWithinKeepsEveryValue`). An `n` of one or less makes each value a chunk. With all three operators, a `d` of zero or less keeps every value.
4. **Time at the arrival.** A `Seq` is a push iterator that one task runs, so each operator decides when a value arrives. It reads the clock it is given then: `Elapsed` since the time it keeps, which on `time.Host` reads only the monotonic clock, as Go's `time.Since` does. That kept time then moves on by the elapsed amount (`Time.Add`), so only the first value, and the first value of each chunk, reads `Now`. The clock is a value, not a need, because a `Seq` carries no row. The producer that sleeps on the context's `Scheduler` between values, and the clock that `Scheduler` moves, give the times. The laws drive a `time.Manual`: before each value, the source moves the clock on by that value's gap. Each law compares an operator with a reference model over the arrival times, and each operator is a `Seq` that stops (`ThrottleStops`, `DebounceStops`, `GroupedWithinStops`; [Std laws](#std-laws) 5). Lower `TestStdStreamLawsRun` runs them.
5. **Debounced** is the debounce with a timer. A `Seq` has no second task to run one, so `Debounced` works over queues and runs as a task of its own: `task.Go() { stream.Debounced(src, d, out) }`. It holds the last value of `src`. Each new value replaces the held one and reads the context's `time.Clock`. One `timeout.Deadline`, armed for `d` at a burst's first value, bounds the waits (`timeout.PopBefore`; [runtime.md, Timeouts](runtime.md#timeouts) 11). When it fires, a held value that arrived `d` or more ago leaves, and a newer one re-arms it for the rest of its `d`, so a burst arms about one alarm per `d`, not one per value. When `d` passes with no new value, it pushes the held value to `out`, so the value leaves at its arrival plus `d` while `src` is open and silent. It never pushes while a burst goes on. When `src` ends, it pushes the held value at once and ends `out`. A value that arrives at a deadline's own instant either replaces the held value or follows it, and is never lost (runtime.md Timeouts 4). It fails `Closed` when `out` is closed first. Law `DebouncedMatchesModel` checks the values and the time each one leaves against a model, on a `time.Manual` with three tasks (producer, `Debounced`, reader); its windows are odd and its gaps even, so no arrival falls on a deadline. A `d` of zero or less keeps every value.
6. *Not taken from Effect:* an operator as a method of a `Stream` effect value run by the fiber runtime (W2, W14). An operator here is a function of a `Seq`, or for `Debounced`, a loop between two queues. `throttle`'s cost function and burst (`shape`) are not taken either.

*Prior art:* Effect's `Stream.throttle`, `Stream.debounce` and `Stream.groupedWithin`; RxJS `throttleTime`, `debounceTime` and `bufferTime`; Elixir's `Stream.chunk_every`, together with `GenStage` and `Broadway` batchers (`batch_size`, `batch_timeout`), which close a batch at a size or a time as `GroupedWithin` does. Gleam's `gleam/yielder` has no timed operators; `gleam_otp` leaves timers to processes. ReScript leaves them to JavaScript's `setTimeout`. In Go, a channel and a `time.Timer` give the same operators over goroutines. Each of these needs a runtime or a second task; here the operator is one loop on the caller's task.

## Packages by origin

- **Same as Go:** `strings`, `strconv`, `utf8`, `slices`, `maps`, `cmp`, `math`, `bits`, `csv` (Go's `encoding/csv`), `hex`, `sha256`, `crc32`, `time`, `rand`, `os`, `fs`, `net`, `sql`.
- **Same name, extended:**
  - `http`: client, handlers, routes, server limits;
  - `json`: `Codec`, `Decode`, `Encode`, `ParseX`/`Wire`;
  - `base64`: Go's two alphabets as the union `Encoding`;
  - `sync`: `Ref`, `TxRef`, `Atomically`, `Latch`;
  - `testing`: `T`, `VirtualClock`, `Discard`, `Idle`, `Point`, `At`.
- **New:** `hmac`, `sha512`, `sha1`, `uuid` and `rand.Secure` (keyed digests, secure random bytes and RFC 9562 ids, std-spike D3), `decimal` (exact decimals, a 128-bit coefficient, std-spike D2), `set` (a value set over a Map, std-spike D1), `cache` (a capacity, a life and an idle time, one lookup per key), `tasks`, `schedule` (the schedule algebra, with laws), `retry`, `timeout`, `trace`, `log`, `metric`, `batch`, `actor` (the live machine runner: `StartMachine`, `Send`, `Ask`, `State`, `Stop`, `Changes`, `Dropped`, `Feed`, `Can`), `machines` (the runs of a machine value: the pure `Run` and `From`, `RunTimed` with state timers on the context's `time.Clock` and `Scheduler`, `Every` for a timer whose signal carries the clock's reading when it fires, `Start` for a machine with an input, `Next`, `Initial`, `Can`), `html`, `lint` (a rule pack in Ergo: the fact snapshot, `Serve`, `Manifest`; [lint](#lint)), `pubsub` (a hub that fans values out to subscriptions: [PubSub and send strategies](#pubsub-and-send-strategies)), `text` (pointer-free spans of a text: [Text spans](#text-spans)), `llm` (the contract of a language model and its providers: [llm](#llm)).
- **New:** `civil` (zone-less dates, times of day, periods and spans; Go's `cloud.google.com/go/civil` name), `tz` with `tz/iana` and `tz/host` (zones, std's own IANA copy, the host's zone files; Go's `Location` renamed zone), `tasks`, `schedule` (the schedule algebra, with laws), `retry`, `timeout`, `trace`, `log`, `metric`, `batch`, `actor` (the live machine runner: `StartMachine`, `Send`, `Ask`, `State`, `Stop`, `Changes`, `Dropped`, `Feed`, `Can`), `machines` (the runs of a machine value: the pure `Run` and `From`, `RunTimed` with state timers on the context's `time.Clock` and `Scheduler`, `Start` for a machine with an input, `Next`, `Initial`, `Can`), `html`.
- **New:** `ansi` (styles, colours and `Strip`, painted where `Colors` is on).

## llm

Package `llm` is the contract of a language model (wf10 L1) and its policy providers (`Retry`, `Fallback`, `Metered`, wf10 L2); `llm/memory` holds its in-memory providers, and `llm/recorded` records and replays a model (wf10 L3). The vendor packages, `Ask` and `llm/agent` come in later units of the same lane and add their rows here.

| Declaration | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `type Model { Generate(r Request) Turn fails …, Stream(r Request, put func(d Delta) fails SinkFailed) Turn fails … \| SinkFailed }` | A model: the whole reply, or the reply with each piece given to `put` as it comes. The row of each operation lists the nine failures. |
| `type Request { System, Messages []Message, Tools []ToolSpec, Choice Choice, Format ResponseFormat, MaxTokens int }` | One ask. `Choice = Auto \| NoTools \| AnyTool \| Named{Name}`; `ResponseFormat = Prose \| JSON{Schema}`. |
| `type ToolSpec { Name, Description, Input jsonschema.Encoded }` | A tool the model may call: its name, what it does, and the schema of its arguments, checked and with its text written once. |
| `type UnknownTool { Name }`, `type BadArguments { Name, Reason }` | A call of a tool set that names no tool, or whose arguments are not the tool's: what a tool set's `Parse` fails with (`ergo gen llm`). |
| `type Message = User{Text} \| Assistant{Text, Calls []Call} \| Result{CallId, Text, Failed}` | One entry of a transcript. |
| `type Turn { Text, Calls []Call, Stop Stop, Usage Usage }`, `type Stop = Ended \| ToolUse \| Length \| Blocked` | A whole reply. |
| `type Delta = Text{Text} \| Reasoning{Text} \| CallStart{Index, Id, Name} \| CallArgs{Index, Text} \| CallEnd{Index}` | One piece of a streamed reply. |
| `type Failure = RateLimited{After Option[int]} \| Overloaded \| Unauthorized \| Refused{Reason} \| Truncated{Text} \| Transport{Reason} \| InvalidRequest{Reason} \| InvalidResponse{Reason} \| UnsupportedSchema{Reason}` | Every way an ask fails. |
| `func Fold(ds []Delta) Draft`, `type Draft { Text, Reasoning, Calls []Call, Open []Call }` | The reply so far: the pieces joined, each call's argument pieces joined by its `Index`, the ended calls apart from the open ones. |
| `func SafetyOf(f Failure) Safety`, `type Safety = Safe \| Unknown \| Unsafe` | Whether an ask that failed may be asked again. |
| `func Valid(ms []Message) bool` | Whether a transcript may be sent: each `Result` answers a call of the `Assistant` message before it, once, and every call is answered before the next message of the person or the model. |
| `Retry{Inner Model, Schedule schedule.Schedule, Clock time.Clock, Sleep Scheduler}` | `Inner`, asked again after a `Safe` or `Unknown` failure while the schedule continues and no delta reached the caller, waiting the schedule's delay or the vendor's `After`, the longer one. |
| `Fallback{Members []Model}` | The first member that gives a turn, each asked once in order while no delta reached the caller; with all failed, the last failure. |
| `Metered`, `NewMetered(inner Model) Metered`, `Used(m Metered) Usage` | `Inner`, with the sum of the usage of the turns it gave. |
| `func Raise(f Failure) Turn fails …` | `f` as the failure of an operation: a provider that holds a failure as a value gives it back. |
| `memory.Scripted{Replies []Reply}`, `type Reply = Says{Deltas, Stop, Usage} \| Cuts{Deltas} \| Breaks{Deltas, Failure} \| Fails{Failure}` | A `Model` that plays a script: the reply to a request is the one numbered by the `Assistant` messages of its transcript. `Breaks` streams its deltas, then fails. |
| `recorded.Record`, `NewRecord(inner Model) Record`, `Exchanges(r Record) []Exchange`, `Lines(r Record) string` | `Inner`, keeping each exchange it answers: the request's `Digest`, the deltas, and `End = Answered{Turn} \| Failed{Failure}`; `Lines` is the JSON-lines file of them. |
| `recorded.Replay`, `NewReplay(xs []Exchange) Replay`, `Read(text string) []Exchange fails Unreadable`, `Write(xs []Exchange) string` | A `Model` that answers the k-th ask of a request with the k-th exchange recorded for its digest, deltas streamed; an ask with none left fails `InvalidRequest` with `Diverged(digest)`. |
| `memory.Sequence{Replies []Reply}`, `NewSequence(replies []Reply) Sequence`, `Asks(s Sequence) int` | A `Model` whose k-th ask of a request, by each operation, gets reply k: a retry gets the next reply, and `Asks` counts what was asked. |
| `memory.Held{Reply Reply}`, `Hold(reply Reply) Held`, `Release(h Held, n int)`, `Open(h Held)`, `Next(h Held) HeldStep`, `type HeldStep = Waits{At int} \| Gave{At int}` | A `Model` that plays one reply as `Scripted` does, but whose `Stream` gives each delta only once the test released it: `Release` lets the next `n` through, `Open` all the rest. `Next` waits for the stream's next step: it waits at delta `At`, or gave delta `At`, so a test waits on an event, never on time. `Generate` gives no delta and does not wait. It reads no clock (wf10 LLM-HELD). |

1. **Purpose** (Effect `effect/ai` `LanguageModel`, unstable there). One program text that asks any vendor's model, with typed tool calls and typed failures, and that its tests run on a script with no network and no key.
2. **A model is two operations** (Effect `LanguageModel.make`, the Vercel AI SDK's `doGenerate` and `doStream`, BAML). A provider is a package with a type that implements `Model`; a program binds one in `cmd/<name>` with `context`. There is no `"vendor/model"` string resolved by the compiler (it would be a std privilege) and no registry: a program that picks a model by name writes a `Map[string, Model]` of its own.
3. **One closed stream vocabulary** (Effect `Response` parts, Vercel's stream parts, smaller). `Delta` has five variants, and the pure `Fold` joins them. `Fold` is the reference of every stream law: a provider's turn is the `Fold` of what it streamed (`StreamReturnsItsFold`), the pieces' cuts do not matter (`FoldIsChunkFree`), and the argument pieces of two calls never join (`FoldKeepsCallsApart`). `Fold` keeps the pieces and joins each text once, so a reply of n pieces costs one copy of its bytes, not one per piece. Files, sources and citations are not in this version.
4. **A stream with no terminal event is a failure** (BAML). `Truncated{Text}` holds the text that came, and a reply with a call still open is never a turn (`TruncatedIsNotATurn`). `Stream` and `Generate` agree (`StreamFoldsToGenerate`): the law's two sides are two asks of the provider.
5. **Failures are a closed union with a class** (Effect `AiError`'s `isRetryable`, BAML's `retry_safety`). `SafetyOf` matches every failure, so a new failure does not build until it is classed. `Safe`: the vendor did no work and asks to come back (`RateLimited`, `Overloaded`). `Unknown`: the vendor may have done work (`Transport`, `Truncated`, `InvalidResponse`); a retry is allowed only before the first delta reaches the caller. `Unsafe`: the same ask fails the same way (`RequestFaultsAreUnsafe`). `RateLimited.After` is a count of milliseconds: a `duration` has no wire form yet, and a recording holds the failure.
6. **The transcript rule is a function** (every vendor refuses a result without its call). `Valid` runs before a send, so a bad transcript is the caller's error, not a vendor's `InvalidRequest` (`AnsweredCallsAreValid`).
7. **The test provider is data** (Effect's arrays of parts, Vercel's `MockLanguageModelV4`, TanStack AI's `fakeText`, BAML's `from_chunks`). `memory.Scripted` is a value with no state, so a replayed transcript is answered as it was. A `Says` that leaves a call open or gives two calls one id is a reply no vendor may send, and both operations fail it `InvalidResponse`, so the contract's laws hold for every script (`CutIsTruncated` holds the cut). `memory.Held` is that data with a gate the test opens (the Vercel AI SDK's `simulateReadableStream` under fake timers; Effect's `TestClock`-driven streams): a test of what a caller does while a reply is still arriving (a cancel, a timeout on a virtual clock) releases the deltas one by one and looks in between. The stream waits at each delta, as `Next` tells, until its release, and then gives it, in the reply's order (`ReleasedArriveInOrder`: the steps are `Waits{0}`, `Gave{0}`, `Waits{1}`, …, so a delta given before its release breaks the law on its first step); an open `Held` streams as `Scripted` does (`OpenLetsAllThrough`), and `llm.Model`'s member laws run on it under `lawHeldOpen` (wf10 LLM-HELD).
8. **Policies are providers** (Effect `ExecutionPlan` and `Schedule`, the Vercel AI SDK's `maxRetries`, BAML's `retry_policy` and `fallback` clients, LiteLLM's fallbacks, yielded/agent's model retry "before content arrives"). `Retry`, `Fallback` and `Metered` each implement `Model` over a `Model`, so they nest (`Metered{Retry{Fallback{…}}}`) and a program binds the stack in one `context`; the contract's laws run for each. `Retry` reads `SafetyOf`: it never asks again after an `Unsafe` failure (`RetryFollowsSafety`), waits at least `RateLimited.After` (`RetryHonoursAfter`; the Vercel SDK's retry reads `retry-after`), and neither it nor `Fallback` asks again once a delta reached the caller (`NoReplayAfterFirstDelta`): the caller has shown it, and BAML and yielded/agent stop there too. `Generate` of each is its `Stream` with a sink that drops the deltas, so the two agree under a failure after the first delta. `Retry` reads its clock only for a schedule that reads the elapsed time (`schedule.Timed`: an `UpTo` or a `Cron`), at the first ask's start and at each failure (`RetryKeepsUpTo`); by any other schedule an ask reads no clock (wf10 LLM-FLOOR). `Retry` holds its clock and scheduler as fields, not as needs, so a program can hold two policies on two clocks and a test binds one `testing.VirtualClock` to both. `Metered` keeps the sum in a `sync.Ref` its copies share (`UsageAdds`) and gives what its inner model gives (`MeteredIsTransparent`); a failure adds nothing, since a `Failure` reports no usage. `llm.Model`'s member laws run for each policy under named contexts (types.md, Laws 10): `llm/memory`'s `lawRetried`, `lawFallenBack`, `lawMetered` and `lawStacked` bind a policy over a script that reaches text, a whole call, a failure after a delta, a failed ask and a cut reply, and `llm/recorded`'s `lawRecording` binds a `Record` over it, since no generator draws a policy's inner `Model` or `Retry`'s `Schedule` (wf10 LLM-LAWCTX). The laws run on `memory.Sequence`, a model whose replies follow the asks, and sit beside it (`llm` cannot import `llm/memory`).
9. **A recording is a file of exchanges** (VCR cassettes, Ruby's VCR and Go's go-vcr; the Vercel AI SDK's recorded fixtures; BAML's `from_chunks`). A program asks a real model once through `recorded.Record`, keeps its `Lines`, and its tests bind `recorded.Replay` of that file, with no network and no key. An exchange names its request by `Digest`, the hex SHA-256 of a length-prefixed form of every part of the request (`DigestIsTheRequest`), never by the request's text, so a key or a private prompt in a request never reaches the file (`RecordingHoldsNoKey`); go-vcr matches on the request itself and needs a hook to scrub it. `Record` asks its inner model by `Stream` for both operations, so every exchange holds its deltas and a replayed `Stream` streams, whichever operation recorded it (`ReplayIsTheRecording`). Asks of one request are matched by their order among that request's asks, never by their place in the run, so a program whose asks of different requests interleave differently still replays. An ask the recording has no exchange left for has diverged: it fails `InvalidRequest` with `Diverged(digest)`, `Unsafe`, so no policy asks it again (`UnknownRequestDiverges`); a failure of its own (`Diverged{Digest}`) would put a test-only case into every provider's row. The file is JSON lines (`FileIsTheRecording`), one `json.Encode` of an `Exchange` each; the program writes and reads it with `fs`.
10. **Effect parts not taken.** `Layer`-provided models and `Context.Tag` (W4): `context`. `generateText` overloads and `Extract*` type functions: the operation's row says what it fails with. `AiError` classes with getters (W5, W6): the union and `SafetyOf`. `Schema` classes for tool parameters (W7): a tool's schema is a value (`ToolSpec.Input`, a `jsonschema.Encoded`). `Chat` with a `Ref` and `ExecutionPlan`: the loop is a machine (`llm/agent`) and a policy is a provider of `Model`.
11. **Tools are an operation type** (wf10 L7; Effect `@effect/ai` `Toolkit`, the Vercel AI SDK's `tool`). `ergo gen llm` ([api.md, ergo gen llm](api.md#ergo-gen-llm)) projects an Api's hub as `Specs`, `Parse` (a `Call`'s argument text decoded straight into the hub's `Request`), `Result` and `Answer` (the hub's `Outcome` as the `Result` message), so a program's model tools and its MCP tools are one set. A spec's schema is a `jsonschema.Encoded`: only `jsonschema.Encode` makes one, from a checked `Schema`, so no tool carries text that is not a schema, and the text is written once, not per request. A program with no Api writes its specs with `jsonschema.Encode` and matches the names itself.
12. **Ask is a typed function** (wf10 L6; BAML's functions, the Vercel AI SDK's `Output.object`, Effect's `generateObject`, Instructor). `WithSchema(r, s)` is the request `r` asking for a reply that fits the schema `s` (a `jsonschema.Encoded`): its `Format` becomes `JSON{Schema}`, for a vendor with a JSON mode, and its system text ends with the schema's text, for one without; the rest of `r` is kept (`FormatKeepsTheRequest`). `Parse[T](c, reply)` reads the reply as a `T` by `json.DecodeLoose`, so a fenced, commented or cut reply is repaired and every repair is reported in the `json.Read[T]`'s `Fixes`; a reply with no value that repairs to a `T` fails `NoFit{Reply, Path, Reason}`, which holds the reply itself (`ParseDecodesOrFails`). No default value is ever returned, so a program sees each reply it cannot use. `Ask[T](c, s, r)` is one `Model.Generate` of `WithSchema(r, s)` and `Parse` of its text (`memory.AskIsParseOfTheReply`); a model's failure fails `Ask` as it is (`memory.AskFailsAsTheModel`), and a reply that stopped at its length is never parsed, even when its text repairs to a value (`memory.LengthStopIsNotParsed`): the loose reader would close it and hand back a value the model did not finish. `Format` and `Parse` are pure, so a program tests its prompt and its parse with no model, and the text a codec writes fits the schema and parses back with no fix (`FormatAgreesWithCodec`). A generic `T`'s failure row names only `WireWrongKind`, so its path is `NoFit.Path`; a missing member or an unknown tag is reported on the whole reply (`Path` ""). *Not in this unit:* `AskStream[T]` (partial values as a stream; it waits for a partial decoder), and asking again with the `NoFit` (Instructor's re-ask): a program writes that loop over `Ask`, as a policy is a provider. *Prior art:* BAML's schema-aligned parsing scores its repairs and picks a value; Ergo reports them and picks the first that decodes (json, Loose reader). Instructor (Python, Elixir's `instructor_ex`) validates strict JSON and asks the model again; the Vercel SDK and Effect validate strict JSON and fail. Gleam and ReScript have no model contract.
13. **Laws.** `Model.StreamFoldsToGenerate`, `Model.StreamReturnsItsFold`, `Model.TruncatedIsNotATurn`, `Model.CallsAreWhole`, `FoldIsChunkFree`, `FoldKeepsCallsApart`, `RequestFaultsAreUnsafe`, `AnsweredCallsAreValid`, `FormatAgreesWithCodec`, `FormatKeepsTheRequest`, `ParseDecodesOrFails`, `memory.AskIsParseOfTheReply`, `memory.LengthStopIsNotParsed`, `memory.AskFailsAsTheModel`, `memory.CutIsTruncated`, `memory.RetryFollowsSafety`, `memory.RetryHonoursAfter`, `memory.RetryKeepsUpTo`, `memory.NoReplayAfterFirstDelta`, `memory.FallbackIsFirstSuccess`, `memory.UsageAdds`, `memory.MeteredIsTransparent`, `recorded.ReplayIsTheRecording`, `recorded.FileIsTheRecording`, `recorded.DigestIsTheRequest`, `recorded.UnknownRequestDiverges` and `recorded.RecordingHoldsNoKey` ([Std laws](#std-laws)); lower `TestStdLLMLawsRun` runs them, the contract's over `memory.Scripted` and `memory.Sequence`. The contract's laws run for every provider that implements `Model`, a program's own included.

*Prior art:* Effect `effect/ai` (`LanguageModel`, `Response`, `AiError`), the Vercel AI SDK (`LanguageModelV4`), TanStack AI, BAML (terminal events, retry safety), yielded/agent (the loop's words, in `llm/agent`). Among the compile-to-host languages, Gleam, ReScript and Elixir have no model contract in their std; Elixir's LangChain and Instructor libraries keep a model behaviour (`ChatModel` callbacks) and a delta struct merged by a function (`MessageDelta.merge_delta`), the same split as `Delta` and `Fold`.

## Schedule

Std schedule, retry and timeout. Package `schedule` says when to run work again and when to stop: the policy of a retry, a repeat, or a restart budget. `tasks.Retry` and `tasks.Repeat` run work by one.

| Declaration | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `type Schedule = …` | A policy. Its variants are unexported, so only the functions below make one. |
| `type Next = Continue \| Stop`, `type Continue { After duration }`, `type Stop {}` | A decision: run again after `After`, or stop. |
| `func Spaced(d duration) Schedule`, `func Forever() Schedule` | Continue forever, after `d` or with no delay. |
| `func Exponential(base duration, factor float64, cap duration) Schedule` | Continue forever; after run `n` the delay is `base × factor^(n−1)`, at most `cap`. |
| `func Recurs(n int) Schedule` | Continue after runs 1 to `n`, then stop. |
| `func UpTo(s Schedule, limit duration) Schedule` | `s`, but stop where a run would start more than `limit` after the first run began. |
| `func Jitter(s Schedule, low float64, high float64, seed uint64) Schedule` | `s`, each delay times a factor in `[low, high)` drawn from `seed` and the run count. |
| `func And(a Schedule, b Schedule) Schedule`, `func Or(a Schedule, b Schedule) Schedule` | Continue while both continue, after the longer delay; continue while either continues, after the shorter delay of those that continue. |
| `func Cron(e cron.Expr, z tz.Zone, start time.Time) Schedule` | Continue at each firing of `e` in `z` (Effect `Schedule.cron`): after a run, wait until `cron.Next(e, z, start + elapsed)`; stop when `e` fires no more. `start` is the instant the first run began, so the value stays pure (law `CronWaitsForAFiring`; std-spike D4). |
| `func Decide(s Schedule, runs int, elapsed duration) Next` | The decision after `runs` runs (1 for the first), `elapsed` after the first run began. |
| `func Timed(s Schedule) bool` | Whether `Decide` reads `elapsed`: `s` holds an `UpTo` or a `Cron`. With an untimed schedule, `tasks.Retry`, `tasks.Repeat`, and `llm.Retry` do not read the clock (law `UntimedIgnoresElapsed`). |
| `func Retry[T, E, R](s Schedule, block func() T fails E needs R) T fails E needs R \| Scheduler \| time.Clock` (package `tasks`) | Run `block` until it gives a value; after a failure, `Decide` continues or stops, and on Stop `Retry` fails with that run's failure. |
| `func Repeat[T, E, R](s Schedule, block func() T fails E needs R) T fails E needs R \| Scheduler \| time.Clock` (package `tasks`) | Run `block`, and again while `Decide` continues; give the last value. A failure ends `Repeat`. |

1. **Purpose** (Effect `Schedule`). A program retries a call that fails for a while (a network, a lock), polls, or limits how often a supervisor restarts a worker. It needs one value that says how long to wait and when to give up, that it can test without waiting.
2. **A pure value.** A `Schedule` is data: a tree of the combinators above. `Decide` is a pure function of the schedule, the run count and the elapsed time, so a test folds it with no clock, and a law states each combinator. The schedule keeps no state between decisions: the run count and the elapsed time are the whole input, and the jitter draw is SplitMix64 of `seed + runs` (inferred: equal to Effect's state for every combinator here, since none of them reads its last output).
3. **One clock.** `Retry` and `Repeat` read the context's `time.Clock` only for a timed schedule: once before the first run and once per schedule decision. Both still declare `time.Clock` because the schedule is a value. They sleep on the context's `Scheduler` for either kind of schedule. When both providers share one timeline, as with `testing.VirtualClock`, the schedule's elapsed time includes both the block's work and its delays.
4. **Totality.** Each constructor takes every argument: a duration below zero is zero (as Go's `time.Sleep` reads it), an exponential factor below 1 (or NaN) is 1, a `Recurs` count below zero is zero, and each jitter bound is moved into `[0, 1000]` with `high` at least `low`. Each part of a jittered delay saturates at 4.6e18 ns, so their sum does not overflow.
5. **UpTo never starts late.** Effect's `upTo(d)` stops once the elapsed time passes `d`, so its last delay may start a run after `d`. `UpTo(s, limit)` stops where the delay would start the run after `limit` (law `UpToNeverPassesLimit`).
6. **Jitter is seeded.** The draw comes from the seed in the value, not from a `Random` capability, so `Retry` needs no `Random` and a test replays it. To keep many clients apart, each draws its seed once from `Random`. The jittered delay is the sum of two scaled durations, not `d × (low + span × u)`: no float product is added to, so no Go target can fuse a multiply-add and round another way (the model test compares exact nanoseconds).
7. **No predicate over the failure.** In `Retry[T, E, R]`, `E` is an error row, not a type ([types.md, Generics](types.md#generics) 1), so no function can take the failure as an argument, and `Retry` has no `while` predicate. A failure that must not be retried is caught in `block` and given as a value of its result.
8. **Re-raise.** `Retry`'s body catches `block`'s failure with `case err:` and, on Stop, returns `err`: a catch binding over a row parameter is a failure when returned ([rows.md, Catch](rows.md#catch) 7).
9. **Effect parts not taken.** `Schedule<Out, In, R>`'s three type parameters, its outputs and its input pipelining (`Schedule.passthrough`, `andThen`, `collectAllOutputs`, `whileInput`): no combinator here reads an input or an output, and a schedule's meaning is a function of the run count and the time (see 7 for the failure). Its fiber-backed step (W14) and `Schedule.driver` (an effect-returning state machine): `Decide` is a pure call. `pipe` and dual APIs (W2, W3): `s.And(t).UpTo(d)` by the dot form. Cron and calendar schedules (`Schedule.cron`, `windowed`) are not built.
10. **Laws.** `CronWaitsForAFiring`, `RecursStopsAfter`, `UpToNeverPassesLimit`, `UntimedIgnoresElapsed`, `ExponentialUnderCap`, `JitterInBounds`, `AndIsMaxOrIsMin`, and `tasks`'s `RetryFollowsDecide` and `RepeatFollowsDecide` ([Std laws](#std-laws)); lower `TestStdScheduleLawsRun` runs them on generated arguments. The task laws count clock reads as well as comparing the result and elapsed time. Lower `TestScheduleAgreesWithModel` compares `Decide`, lowered, with a Go reference model on generated schedules (every combinator, every clamp, both ends of each decision; `ERGO_SCHEDULE_SEED` replays a seed).

*Prior art:* Effect's `Schedule` (the reference for the intent); Go has no std backoff, and `cenkalti/backoff` keeps a mutable `ExponentialBackOff` with a randomization factor and a `MaxElapsedTime`; Erlang/OTP supervisors count restarts in a window (`intensity`, `period`); Elixir's `Retry` library composes delay streams (`exponential_backoff |> cap |> expiry`), a lazy list of delays, close to `Decide` folded over the runs; Gleam and ReScript have no schedule in their std.

## Bench statistics

Std `bench` is the referee of `ergo bench` ([specs/bench.md](../specs/bench.md)). `bench/stats.ergo` (STROBE BN1) is the statistics, `bench/referee.ergo` (BN2) the referee machine (items 6 and 7), and `bench/report.ergo` (BN5) the table, the receipt, the block and the card (item 8): pure Ergo, no clock, no process.

| Declaration | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `func Decide(pairs []Pair, k Kind, c Claim, level float64) Verdict` | Judge one series of paired blocks: refuse it, compare an exact counter exactly, or read the Hodges-Lehmann interval at `level` against the claim. |
| `type Verdict = Better \| Worse \| Same \| NoWorse \| Unclear \| Underpowered \| Refused` | What the referee says. Every variant but `Refused` carries a `Difference`; `Refused` carries a `Refusal` and no number. |
| `type Kind = Exact \| Counted \| Timed`; `type Claim = Change \| NoWorseThan`; `type Design = Planned \| Fixed` | The kind of a counter, the question asked, and the plan of looks (`Level`, `Looks`, `IsLook`). |
| `func SignedRankP(d []float64) float64` | The two-sided Wilcoxon signed-rank p-value, zeros dropped, ties at their mid-rank, from the exact null distribution of those ranks. |
| `func HodgesLehmann(d []float64, level float64) Estimate` | The median of the Walsh averages and the narrowest symmetric interval of the sorted averages whose exact coverage reaches `level` (`[min, max]`, with its coverage, when none does). |
| `func Holm(ps []float64, alpha float64) []bool` | Holm's step-down over the p-values of the secondaries. |
| `Median`, `Spread`, `Needed`, `PairsFor`, `Quantile`, `MinPairs`, `MaxPairs` | The parts of the pairs-needed projection and the limits of a series. |

1. **Purpose.** A speed claim is a paired verdict with its interval, or a refusal with its reason; never a ratio of two medians with no interval. Each pair holds one block of each side, so a host whose load drifts moves both values of a pair, and the test reads only the differences.
2. **Method.** The method is strobe's (0.32.0, SPEC §9.3), and [specs/bench.md](../specs/bench.md) states it with the three changes Ergo makes: the equivalence bound is symmetric in log space (`ln(1 + Bound)` both ways, so swapping the sides mirrors every verdict); a series holds at most 400 pairs, so every distribution is exact and no code path uses a normal approximation; and an exact counter that moves is refused (`CounterUnstable`), not compared like a timed one.
3. **Totality.** `Decide` takes every input: more than 400 pairs is `TooManyPairs`, a NaN or an infinity is `NotFinite` with its pair, fewer than 6 pairs is `FewPairs`. A bound or a margin below 0 is 0. Every counter is better lower.
4. **Laws** ([Std laws](#std-laws)): `MatchesEnumeration` holds `SignedRankP` to a count over every sign pattern of up to 9 tied and zero differences; `IntervalCoverageIsExact` holds the interval's coverage to a count over every sign pattern of up to 6 differences; `IntervalHoldsEstimate`, `Mirror`, `ScaleFree`, `FewPairsNeverDecide`, `AllEqualIsSame`, `ExactIsExact`, `NoWorseReadsTheInterval`, `HolmRejectsNoMore` and `PlannedLevelIsPocock`; `NullMatchesReference`, `SignedRankPMatchesReference` and `HodgesLehmannMatchesReference` hold the null tables, which are built only up to the tail read (by counts scaled once, the doubled ranks over their common divisor, one untied half table shared by a Decide's interval and p-value), bit for bit to the whole table built by halving (`refNull`, kept as the reference; BENCH-LAW-COST). These laws draw at most 12 pairs, so that each decides inside check time's budget; lower `TestStdBenchMatchesReference` holds `SignedRankP` and `HodgesLehmann` to BN1's code (the whole tables, verbatim) with `==` from 13 to `MaxPairs` pairs, untied reals and runs of ties longer than any law's series, at levels from 0 to 99.9%, as a seeded property of a built program (BENCH-PROP). Lower `TestStdBenchLawsRun` builds the package's own source as one test build and runs each law as written there, so no restated copy can drift.
5. **Effect parts not taken.** Effect has no statistics and no benchmark module at the pin; its `Metric` histograms summarize one run and judge nothing.
6. **The referee** (`bench/referee.ergo`, STROBE BN2) is the sequential design of one run as a machine, `type Referee machine[Judging, Measured]`, and as the driver `func Judge(p Plan) Result needs Meter`. `Next` gives the block to run (`Run{Block}`) or the result (`Finished{Result}`); `Record` takes the block's samples. Block 0 is base's warm block and block 1 head's, both discarded; pair k runs base first when k is even and head first when it is odd. After each pair that is a look of the design, the referee judges the primary with `Decide`; once the primary has its word, the secondaries are judged at each look. A decided series keeps the verdict and the pairs of its look. A planned series whose 50% projection needs more pairs than the last look stops as `Underpowered`. A `Lost` sample refuses its series from that pair on and no other; a refused primary ends the run, and a secondary that has no word by then has `None`. The run ends at the first look where every series has its word, or at the last look, where an undecided series says `Unclear`. The secondaries that say `Better` or `Worse` from an interval are one family under Holm's step-down at 5%; one it does not reject says `Unclear`. A `Fixed` design with fewer than `MinPairs` or more than `MaxPairs` pairs is refused before any block runs. A run asks for at most `Bound(design)` blocks.
7. **Meter** is the operation type `Meter { Measure(b Block, series []Series) []Sample }` with the law `ReadsEverySeries`; a sample is `Got{Value}` or `Lost{Why Refusal}`, and a sample a meter leaves out is `Lost{Why: NoMetric}`, never 0. `Scripted` is its provider for tests (seeded noise, planted effects, warm blocks slower, planted refusals; no process and no clock); the host meter is STROBE BN3's. Laws `MatchesReference` (a reference model that recomputes every word from the full list of blocks, carrying no state between blocks), `AlternatesOrder`, `MachineIsJudge` and `LoneSecondaryKeepsItsWord`, over plans of a Fixed design of at most 6 pairs and at most one secondary, so each decides at check time. Lower `TestStdBenchJudgeMatchesReference` holds the rest as seeded properties of a built program: `Judge` against the reference model and the machine against `Judge` over every drawn plan (Planned's looks at 8 to 40 pairs, Fixed up to 30 pairs and 401, up to five secondaries) and over Planned plans of three to six series, and Holm's family at one to five secondaries (JUDGE-PROP).
8. **The report** (`bench/report.ergo`, STROBE BN5). A `Receipt` is one run as `bench.v1` publishes it (`Schema`, `Id`, `Experiment`, `Host`, `Builds`, `Cases` of `Case{Name, Unit, Plan, Result}`). `Encode` and `Decode` are the derived JSON codec ([json](#json)); `Decode` refuses another schema (`Unreadable`). `Table` prints one line per series: the word, the difference per bench unit (a percent on the log scale), and its proof (the interval with its coverage, or `exact`, and the pairs); a refusal prints its reason and no value; a nonzero value never prints as zero (its decimals follow its interval's width, and three significant digits when those would read 0). `Markdown` is the block `<!-- ergo:bench ID v1 begin sha256:DIGEST -->`, the table and the receipt, and `<!-- ergo:bench ID v1 end -->`, the digest that of the text between the two lines. `ReadBlock` fails `Edited` when the text is not the one its digest names, and `Update` replaces the block of a receipt's id in place, or appends one, and refuses a block a hand edit broke. `Card` is an SVG card of a case's primary, with the id and digest of the block it comes from. Every writer publishes `Scrubbed(r)`: each `/home/NAME` and `/Users/NAME` becomes `~`. Laws `BlockRoundTrips`, `EditIsRefused`, `UpdateReplacesItsBlock`, `NumberHasItsProof`, `RefusedHasNoValue`, `NonzeroNeverPrintsZero`, `NoHomePath` and `CardNamesItsBlock`, over receipts of referee runs on `Scripted`.

*Prior art:* strobe (Rust, `src/stats.rs`) is the source of the method; `golang.org/x/perf/benchstat` tests unpaired samples (Mann-Whitney, inferred, not fetched); MoonBit's `moon bench` prints a mean and a deviation and compares nothing; Gleam, ReScript and Elixir have no paired verdict in their toolchains (Elixir's Benchee summarizes runs).

## Process

Package `process` runs a block as a process: a child task whose end is one typed value, never the caller's crash. It is plain Ergo over `task.Go`, `task.ExitOf` ([runtime.md](runtime.md#tasks) Tasks 13) and `timeout.After`.

1. `process.Run(l Limits) { … }` runs the block as a child task of the caller and gives its value. It fails with the block's typed failure, `OverTime{Limit}` when `l.Time` passes first on the `Scheduler`, and `Crashed{Reason}` when a defect ends the block, `Reason` being the report's text of the defect (`task.Defected`'s). Every exit action and task of the block has ended before `Run` returns, on every path. A stop of the caller stops the block and is the caller's interruption, never an `Exit`. A Go fatal error is no defect (runtime.md, Exit causes 6): it ends the program.
2. `Limits { Time duration }`: a zero field is no bound. `Unlimited` has none. A limit is written `Limits{..Unlimited, Time: 2s}`, so a field added to `Limits` leaves the literal valid. `Exit = OverTime | Crashed` is the process's own ends.
3. `Runner { Play(l Limits, s Script, on Scheduler) Report }` is the contract of a process provider; the `Scheduler` is an argument, since an operation writes no capability row ([types.md](types.md#capabilities) Capabilities 4). A `Script` is data that says what a block does: `Cleanups` exit actions, a wait of `Waits` on the `Scheduler`, then its `End` (`Gives{Value}`, `Fails{Code}`, `Panics{Text}`). A `Report` is how the process `Ended` (`Gives`, `Fails`, `OverTime`, `Crashed`) and how many exit actions had run when `Play` returned (`Cleaned`). `Local` plays with `Run`. Its member laws hold for every provider: `ValueOrFailurePasses`, `DefectIsCrashed`, `TimeIsExpired` (a manual clock; time is the input), `CleanupRunsBeforeReturn`, and `ModelAgrees` (a seeded run of scripts against a fold).

*Prior art:* Erlang's `spawn_monitor` plus `receive {'DOWN', …}` ends one process and reports its reason, and `max_heap_size` and a timer are its limits. Elixir's `Task.Supervisor.async_nolink` with `Task.yield`/`Task.shutdown` does the same. Effect's `Effect.timeout` with `Effect.exit` gives a fiber's `Exit` as a value. Go's `errgroup` with `context.WithTimeout` and a `recover` in the goroutine is the hand form. Gleam (`gleam_otp`'s `process.start` with a monitor), Elixir and ReScript lower no process of their own: the first two inherit the BEAM's, and ReScript has one thread.

## Tasks

Std concurrency. `tasks.Set` is G4 ([runtime.md](runtime.md#g4-tasksset)).

| Declaration | Meaning | Go body |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `func Transform[A, B, E, R](xs []A, limit int, f escaping func(x A) B fails E needs R) []B fails E needs R` | `f` of each element, each call on a task of its own, at most `limit` at once, the results in the order of `xs` (batch 12, C2; built). `f` is escaping: its calls run at the same time ([types.md](types.md#function-values), Function values 2; ESC1). | `TasksTransform(span Site, t *rt.Task, rel Releaser[[]B], xs []A, limit int, f func(*rt.Task, A) (B, error)) ([]B, error)` (task.go), over `rt.MapSpan` (rt tasks.go) |
| `func Race[T, E, R](blocks []func() T fails E needs R) T fails E needs R` | Each block on a task of its own; the first to end decides, and the rest are stopped and waited for (batch 12, C4; built). | `TasksRace(span Site, t *rt.Task, rel Releaser[T], blocks []func(*rt.Task) (T, error)) (T, error)` (task.go), over `rt.RaceSpan` (rt tasks.go) |
| `law TransformEqualsLoop(xs []int, k int)`, `law TransformKeepsOrder(xs []int, k int)`, `law LimitHolds(xs []int, k int)`, `law EarliestWins(ds []int)`, `law LosersStopped(n int)` | The laws ([Std laws](#std-laws)). | `TestTasksLaws` (runtime/v1); `LosersStopped` runs in `ergo test std:tasks` |

1. **Name.** The function is the concurrent form of `slices.Transform` and has its name: `Map` is the prelude's type, which no package redeclares (S108).
2. **Limit.** At most `limit` calls of `f` run at once: a call starts only while fewer run, and when one ends the next element starts. A `limit` below 1 is a defect of the caller, and no call starts.
3. **Order.** The result holds `f(xs[i])` at index `i`, whatever order the calls end in.
4. **Fail fast.** `Transform` waits for whichever call ends first. The first failure it sees ends it: no call starts after it, every call still running is stopped, and all are waited for before `Transform` fails with that failure. A defect in a call, or in the stop of another, is raised in the caller, as `Task.Wait` raises it.
5. **Structured.** Every task `Transform` starts has ended when it returns, on every path. A stop of the calling task stops every call, and `Transform` raises the interruption. The calls share `f`'s providers, which the caller's row binds.
6. **Virtual time.** The caller's wait is one the VirtualClock sees ([Testing](#testing) 2), so a `Transform` over sleeping calls runs in virtual time.

`TestTransformModel` (runtime/v1) runs 300 generated cases on a VirtualClock against a discrete-event model: the results or the failure, the elements started, the most calls at once and the instant `Transform` returns. *Prior art:* Go's `errgroup.Group` with `SetLimit` (the first error cancels the group's context; `Wait` waits for all); Effect's `Effect.forEach(xs, f, { concurrency })`, fail-fast with interruption of the rest; Elixir's `Task.async_stream` with `max_concurrency` and `ordered: true`; Gleam and ReScript have no bounded parallel map in their std (Gleam's OTP `task` awaits one by one). *Effect workarounds replaced:* the `{ concurrency }` options object of one combinator for sequential and concurrent loops (W15) becomes a plain `for` and `Transform`; `Effect.gen` and `yield*` around `f` (W1) become the plain call.

`Race` (Effect's `raceAll`; Go's `select` over result channels with a cancel of the losers):

7. **The first to end decides.** `Race` waits for whichever block ends first. Its value is `Race`'s value, and its failure is `Race`'s failure. Every other block is stopped, and `Race` returns only after each has ended and all exit actions it registered have run. A stop may reach a block before it starts or before it registers an exit action. A loser's failure is dropped (laws R3, R6); a defect in any block is raised in the caller.
8. **Ties.** Blocks that end at the same instant, each with its own exit before the stop reaches it, tie, and the earliest of them in `blocks` wins. On a VirtualClock no time passes while a block can run, so a tie is a tie of virtual instants; a sleep whose deadline came at the instant of the stop ends as a sleep, not as an interruption ([Testing](#testing)). runtime/rt `TestVirtualSleepTieEndsAsSleep` holds this at the one point where both can be ready: a test hook brings the stop between the clock's unlock and the sleeper's select, after its own deadline fired.
9. **Shape.** Ergo has no variadic parameters, so the blocks are one slice. A slice of function values has no Go form a body can take, so `Race`'s Ergo body hands the Go body one function of an index, `func(i int) T { try blocks[i]() }`. `Race` of no blocks is a defect: it would never end. A stop of the calling task stops every block.
10. **No select (decision O13).** A block may finish its work before the stop reaches it, so a race of two `queue` `Pop`s can take a value from the losing queue and drop it. To wait for whichever of several sources has a value, give the task one queue of a union that every source pushes to (the inbox pattern), and `match` on what it pops. Ergo has no `select` construct in batch 12.

`TestRaceModel` (runtime/v1) runs 300 generated races of 1 to 6 blocks, with ties, on a VirtualClock against the model: the earliest-instant block wins (the lowest index on a tie), `Race` returns at that instant, and every block's exit action has run. *Prior art:* Effect's `raceAll` (first to complete; losers interrupted and awaited) and `race` (first success); Go's `select`, which picks at random among ready cases; Kotlin's `select { onReceive }`, an atomic choice that `Race` does not give; Elixir's `Task.yield_many` with `Task.shutdown`. Gleam and ReScript have no race in their std.

`Set` (runtime.md G4; batch 13 tasks-set; built):

| Declaration | Meaning | Go body |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `type Policy = FailFast \| LogFailures \| Restart`, `type Restart { Limit int }`, `type Closed {}` | What a member's failure or defect does to its Set; the failure of `Go` on a Set that admits no more tasks. | `tasks.Closed` is `TasksClosed` (set.go); the policy records are the program's |
| `type Set[E] { Wait() fails E, Stop(), Len() int }`, `func NewSet[E](p Policy) Set[E]` | An owner of any number of tasks, which the calling frame owns. | `*Set` (set.go); `NewSet`'s Ergo body matches the policy and calls `newSet`, whose body is `TasksNewSet(s *Scope, kind, limit int) *Set`; `Wait(t *rt.Task) error`, `Stop(t *rt.Task)`, `Len() int`, `Move(to *Scope) *Set` |
| `func Go[E, R](set Set[E], body escaping func() fails E needs R) fails Closed needs R` | Starts `body` as a member. | `TasksGo(span Site, t *rt.Task, set *Set, body func(*rt.Task) error) error` (set.go) |
| `law NoMemberOutlivesItsFrame(n int)`, `law FinishedAreDropped(n int)`, `law FailFastStopsTheRest(n int)`, `law RestartRunsAgain(n int)`, `Set`'s `law StopEndsEveryMember()` | The laws ([Std laws](#std-laws)). | `TestTasksLaws` (runtime/v1) |

*Intent.* A program that serves (a listener's connections, a supervisor's actors) starts tasks for as long as it runs. It keeps no `Task` per started task: each should end on its own and be forgotten, and none may outlive the code that started it. Effect's `FiberSet` serves this purpose (a fiber leaves the set when it ends; the scope's close interrupts the rest; `join` fails with the first failure), and so does Go's `errgroup.Group` (`Go`, then `Wait` for the first error; `WithContext` cancels the rest). Erlang/OTP's `one_for_one` supervisor adds restarts within an intensity limit.

11. **Owner.** The frame whose call made the Set owns it, by the rule that keeps a `Task` in its frame ([runtime.md, Tasks](runtime.md#tasks); check/taskescape.go): one rule for both owners of tasks (owner q39). The frame's end stops every member and waits for it, with the frame's other entries, last registered first. A return moves the Set to the caller (`open() tasks.Set[E]` returns one). A Set is never a field, an element, a type argument or a capture of a block or another task (S108 "a tasks.Set leaves its function only by return; stop it or return it"). So only the task that made it reaches it, and `Go`, `Wait`, `Stop` and `Len` run on the owner's task.
12. **Members.** `Go` starts `body` as a child of the calling task and returns at once; its site names the member in a defect chain (G2). A member that ends is dropped: `Len` counts the members still running, and the Set keeps no handle of an ended member past the owner's next call (`FinishedAreDropped`).
13. **Policy.** A member's typed failure or defect: under `FailFast`, the first one ends the Set: admission closes, the other members are stopped, and `Wait` gives it (`FailFastStopsTheRest`). Under `LogFailures`, it is written to the program's standard error as main's exit report writes it (`error: …`, or the defect report with its chain), and dropped; the Set goes on. Under `Restart{Limit}`, a defect runs the body again on the same task, up to `Limit` times for each `Go` (`RestartRunsAgain`); a typed failure, and the defect after the last restart, end the Set as under `FailFast`. A negative `Limit` is a defect. A member stopped by its Set or by its owner has no failure under any policy. A restart is immediate with a lifetime `Limit`; the Set does not use the window or backoff of `tasks.Decide`.
14. **Wait.** `Wait` waits until no member runs.
