A `Rename` renames a declaration's family (an operation with its providers' functions) at every site the tables give, and is `Ready{Alpha}` only when the result's tables equal the first under the name map; it is refused `NameTaken`, `OutsideWorkspace` or `Capture` (SEM5's core; [code.md, Rename](code.md#rename)). Laws: [Std laws](#std-laws), rows `code`. *Prior art:* Gleam's compiler records kinded references while it checks; gopls serves references to agents by symbol name; SCIP names symbols by printable strings.

## Graph and codegraph

CI-SELECT GRAPH-STD. Std `graph` is a directed graph over dense node ids, `Graph` built by `Of(size, edges []Edge)` and stored by source in one flat list; what a node stands for lives in the caller's own rows, indexed by the same ids (not `Graph[N, E]`: a node's data is a column the caller already has). Its functions: `Succ`, `Edges`, `Reverse`, `Reach` (what a set of nodes reaches), `Path` (a shortest path, Option), `Components` (strongly connected, each sorted, a component after every one it reaches), `Cycles` (each component that closes a cycle, with one shortest closed path through its least id), `Order` (a topological order, least id first, `None` with a cycle), `Chains(g, min)` (a longest path from each node no edge enters), `Reduce` (the edges another path covers) and `FanOf`. Each states a law against a slow reference: the transitive closure and Floyd-Warshall distances of a drawn graph.

Std `codegraph` reads the rows of [graph.md](graph.md) (`Decode` of `ergo graph export --json`) and answers the questions of CI-SELECT §12.4 as functions of the rows: `Cycles(r, level, tests)` at the `Packages`, `Files` or `Decls` level (`LevelOf`; without tests, an import whose every use comes from a test or a law is left out), `Why(r, a, b)` (the chain of steps by which `a` depends on `b`), `Affected(r, changed)` (every node that depends on a changed one, with the step that reached it) with `Changed(old, new)` (top-level declarations whose digest differs), `Hidden` (a use of another package no file of the user's imports, directly or through a re-exporting let), `Chains`, `Up(r, layers)` (imports that go up a declared layering), `Fan`, `Reduce` and `Cohesion`. A dependency is every edge row but `contains`, and a member to the declaration that holds it. `codegraph` names no package path: a project reads its own graph with the same functions, and `ergo graph` is the linked command line over them (docs/tooling.md, `ergo graph`). Laws: [Std laws](#std-laws), rows `graph` and `codegraph`. *Prior art:* Elixir's `mix xref graph --format cycles` and `--label compile-connected`; Go's `go list -deps` with no library over it; Bazel query's `somepath`, `allpaths` and `rdeps`; Turborepo's `turbo query`.

## Build

CF2 (q10; orchestrator, 2026-11-17). Std `build` (`internal/sketch/std/build`) tells a function what is decided when the program is built. The lowering computes a call of a function whose row is empty and whose arguments are known at build time, and writes its result as a constant ([runtime.md, Build-time folds](runtime.md#i1-interruption-points)); a call whose arguments are partly known is computed as far as its known parts go, a *template* whose holes the program fills when it runs.

1. `InFold() bool` is whether the code runs in a build-time fold: `false` in a built program (`runtime/v1.BuildInFold`), `true` in the evaluator that computes a fold. `Known[T](x T) bool` is whether `x` is known when the program is built: `false` in a built program (`runtime/v1.BuildKnown`); in a fold, `true` unless `x` holds a template's hole. `Hole[T](x T) bool` is whether `x`, as a whole, is a hole: `false` in a built program (`runtime/v1.BuildHole`); a function asks it to place a hole in its result as it is. Any module may call them; std holds no privilege over them.
2. A function that branches on `InFold` or `Known` builds its result in a form a fold can write (one constant, or a template's parts) only in a fold. The rule: its branches give results no program can tell apart, and a law states it by calling both branches. `BranchesAgree` is the rule's worked case, and std html's `StaticIsItsTree` is its use. A fold then changes how fast a program runs, never what it prints.
3. `BuiltKnowsNoFold`: a built program runs in no fold, knows no value at build time and holds no hole.

*Prior art:* C++'s `std::is_constant_evaluated`, Zig's `@inComptime`, GCC's `__builtin_constant_p`.

## HTML

Std html. Owner decision, 2026-10-26: HTML is "Functions", not markup syntax. There is no markup grammar.

```go
import html

func OrderRow(o Order) html.Node {
	return html.Tr([]html.Child{
		html.Class(statusClass(o.Status)),
		html.Td([]html.Child{html.Text(o.Member.Name)}),
		html.Td([]html.Child{html.Int(o.Quantity.Wire())}),
	})
}
```

1. The `html` package (`internal/sketch/std/html`) is ordinary functions in plain Ergo: elements (`Tr`, `Td`, `P`, …), attributes (`Class`, `Href`, …), `Text`, `Int`, `Each`, `When`, `Raw`, `Doc`, `Render` and `Write`. A component is a function that returns `html.Node`. An attribute whose name clashes with an element is `TitleAttr` or `StyleAttr`; `Attribute(name, value)`, `Flag(name)`, `Data` and `Aria` take any name, and a name read from data is written with each byte other than a letter, a digit, `-`, `_`, `.` or `:` as `-` (an empty one as `data-`), so it stays one attribute and never ends the tag.
2. An element takes one slice, `[]Child`, where `Child` is the closed union `Attr | Node`; Ergo has no variadic parameters. The attributes are written in the start tag in their order, wherever they stand among the children. A void element (`Br`, `Hr`, `Img`, `Input`, `Link`, `Meta`, `Source`, `Wbr`) takes only `[]Attr`, so it cannot get children, and is written `<br/>` with no end tag. `Script` and `Style` take `[]Attr` and a `Trusted` body: a parser does not read their body as text, so no `Text` can be put there.
3. Escaping is decided by type. `Text` escapes its string, and so does an attribute's value: `&`, `<`, `>`, `"` and `'` are written `&amp;`, `&lt;`, `&gt;`, `&#34;` and `&#39;` (`html/template`'s set), so any string reads back as itself in text and in a quoted value (law `TextIsInert`). Only an `html.Node` is inserted as markup. `Href`, `Src` and `Action` take an `html.URL`, not a string: `ParseURL(s)` keeps a URL with no scheme or with `http`, `https`, `mailto` or `tel`, and fails `UnsafeURL` for any other, the scheme read as a browser reads it (case, tabs and line breaks inside, controls and spaces before do not hide `javascript:`); `LocalURL(path)` is a link on the same site that never names another host (law `LinksRefuseScripts`). `Raw` takes an `html.Trusted`, which only `html.Trust` builds, so a search for `html.Trust` finds every place a string becomes markup.
4. **Lowering.** `html.Node` is written in one pass into one `strings.Builder`: an element holds its children and a writer that writes its tags around them, `Text` holds its string and escapes it at write time (no writer), so no intermediate string per node is built, and `Each` calls its function as it writes, so no list of nodes is built. A node's markup is its parts' markup in order (law `RenderIsAppend`), and a generated tree's markup parses back to the tree under a reference parser (law `RenderParsesBack`, which draws markup, references, `</style>` and `</script>` in a `Text`, a script's JSON body, `javascript:` links and attribute names from data). A node a build-time fold computes ([runtime.md, Build-time folds](runtime.md#i1-interruption-points)) is its *markup*, one string: html's constructors ask [`build.InFold()`](#build), true in the evaluator that computes a fold and false in a built program, and build the markup only then, so a page's static parts are string constants, as templ's are, and a render with data builds no markup before it writes. `Static(n)` says whether a node is markup (`Int`, `Raw`, an empty `When`, or a folded node). Law `StaticIsItsTree` holds a node's markup the same frozen into one string, written as a tree, and written with its children behind `Each`. A node a fold computes from constants and data the program has only when it runs (a *template*, CF2) is one node of parts: its markup, one string between each two places that hold data, and in those places the data itself, a text escaped when it is written, an attribute value escaped between the markup around it, an attribute, an `Each` or a node of the program's own, so a render writes the static markup as string constants and escapes each datum straight into its one builder, as Marko's and Solid's server compilers and Svelte 5's server output do. An element, a void element and `Doc` build their template in a fold (`build.InFold`), placing a value the build does not know as it is (`build.Known`, `build.Hole`), and splice a template among their children into theirs. `Template(open, kids, mid, close)` is that form, built as a fold builds it, from `Trusted` markup; law `TemplateIsItsTree` holds its markup equal to the element's, and the lowering's property (`TestTemplateAgrees`) holds a program built with templates printing what it prints without them. *Decided (orchestrator, CF1 r1):* the fold is the mark, as C++'s `std::is_constant_evaluated` and Zig's `@inComptime` mark compile-time evaluation; the laws, not a parity row, hold the two forms equal. Reverse by deleting `inFold`'s branches. *Decided by measurement (CF1 r1):* an element keeps WEB0's writer rather than a data node of kind, tag, children and attributes: `Child` is a flattened union, and the data node made each child 2.3 times as large, which cost 2% a render over WEB0 on the probe page (402K against 393K instructions); with the writer it is 392K. A node's writer takes the `*strings.Builder` it writes into; the IR path carries such a function value and refuses only a body that reads through the pointer. `http.HTML(status, node)`, which renders a node into a response, is not built.

*Prior art:* Gleam Lustre (`html.td([], [text(name)])`), whose surface Ergo takes; Elm `Html`, which escapes by type with separate attribute and child lists, merged here into one `[]Child` slice of the union, so an element takes one argument; templ (Go), whose render-function representation Ergo takes, not its syntax; Phoenix HEEx (Elixir), whose compile-time validation Ergo gets from closed types instead of a parser.

## UI

Package `ui` (`internal/sketch/std/ui`) is Ergo over std `arena`: the frame and its retained tree, the patch, view-local cells, loads whose fetches run as tasks, pointer, key and frame events, canvases, virtual rows, text layout, a `Run` loop and the in-process test host. Its laws run in `TestUITreeModel`, `TestUILoadModel`, `TestUIHostModel` and `TestUITextModel` (lower). The desktop host is `hosts/mygo`; the terminal cell host is `examples/cells/host` ([UI cell host](#ui-cell-host)).

```go
func Screen(f *ui.Frame[todo.Signal], l todo.List) {
	ui.Column(f, ui.Plain()) {
		let draft = ui.NewDraft(f, "")
		ui.Field(f, draft, "What needs doing?")
		ui.Submit(f, draft, "Add", todo.Add{Title: draft.Value}) {
			ui.Text(f, "Add")
		}
		ui.Text(f, "\(todo.Left(l)) left")
		ui.Each(f, todo.ItemsOf(l), func(i todo.Item) ui.Key { ui.ByInt(i.ID) }) func(i todo.Item) {
			ui.Check(f, i.Title, i.Done, todo.Toggle{ID: i.ID})
			ui.Pressable(f, "Delete", todo.Remove{ID: i.ID}) {
				ui.Text(f, "Delete")
			}
		}
	}
}
```

1. **A view writes into a frame.** A view is an ordinary function `func(f *ui.Frame[E], s S)`, where `E` is the program's signal type. The primitives `Box`, `Row`, `Column`, `Scroll`, `Pressable` (containers, with a trailing block) and `Text`, `Check`, `Input` (leaves) write one node each into `f`; `Each(f, xs, key) func(x T) { … }` writes a row per element. A view's row says what else it touches (H8b, item 19): `needs` for the services it and its loads use, `fails` for the failures and `ui.Pending` a boundary catches; apart from them it is pure, and writes only through `f`. *Decided (corpus wave Q1, 2026-11-12):* the frame is an explicit borrow, not a capability `needs ui.Slot[E]`, because a capability's type arguments are concrete ([types.md, Capabilities](types.md#capabilities) 7), so no generic function (`ui.Column[E]`, `ui.Run[S, E]`, a program's own `Card[E]`) could name `Slot[E]`. The borrow is Gio's `layout.Context` shape and needs no language change; a later language unit that admits capabilities over type parameters makes the change of signatures mechanical.
2. **A pass.** `Render(f, view, s)` runs `view(f, s)` as one pass and commits it: the frame's retained tree then shows what the pass wrote, and the result is `[]Op[E]`, the changes a host applies in order: `Insert{Id, Parent, Before, Node}`, `Move{Id, Parent, Before}`, `Remove{Id}` (the node and every node below it), `Set{Id, Node}` and `Define{Id, Looks}` (the looks of a new `LookId`, before any node names it: **Looks** below). `Before` names the sibling a node goes in front of, `None` for last. Reused children whose old positions form the longest increasing run stay; each other reused child is one `Move`. `Mirror` is a host's copy built only from patches (`Apply` fails `BadPatch` on an op that does not fit). Its children are lists linked through the nodes, as a DOM's are, so each op reads and writes a fixed number of nodes wherever its `Before` is, and a locator reads each node it visits once; `MirrorReads(m)` counts those reads, and the law `MirrorWorkIsLinear` holds a mount and a `Locate` linear by that count (FK11-g8, FK13-g2: the mirror was a map of nodes with child arrays, which a read through `*Mirror` copied whole, so a mount of n rows and one `Locate` were quadratic). The law `MirrorRejectsBadPatches` sends each op that does not fit (an Insert of a held Id, an op on an Id not held, a `Before` that is the op's own Id, not held, or a child of another parent, a Move to another parent, a Remove of the root, a Set that changes the kind) and requires a `BadPatch`. `Apply` is all or nothing: a patch that fails with `BadPatch` leaves the mirror exactly as it was before the patch, so a typed failure never leaves half a change and a host can resync without guessing. It undoes the ops before the failing one, last first, and a Remove frees its subtree's entries only once the whole patch fits; the law puts each bad op after valid ones (a Set, a Move, an Insert, a Remove of a row and of a column) in one patch and requires the mirror's screen and held nodes unchanged, and the valid ops then to apply as on a mirror that never saw the failed patch. *Decided (orchestrator, 2026-11-15):* all or nothing. Reverse by a rule that names the prefix a failed patch leaves. And `FrameShape`, `TreeShape` and `MirrorShape` give each as values of `Shape[E]`, so a test or an agent compares screens as data.
3. **Identity.** A node's identity among its siblings is the call site that wrote it (the hidden final `CallSite` of every primitive, [types.md, Call sites](types.md#call-sites)), its `Each` row (the `Each`'s site and the row's `Key`), and how many earlier siblings share both. `Key` is `ByInt(n)` or `ByText(s)`: a generic key type cannot be kept in one tree. A node keeps its `Id` across passes while its identity returns under the same parent and its kind (the variant of `Node`) stays.
4. **Events are data.** `Pressable` and `Check` carry the signal value `On` a press sends; `Input` takes `func(t string) E`, the signal of a typed text. `Handle(f, event)` applies an `Event` (`Press{Id}`, `Type{Id, Text}`, `Arrived{Epoch, Signal}`, a fetch's result, whose signal it gives only while `Arrive` holds, `Point{Id, Pointer}`, `KeyDown{Press}` or `Tick{At}`, item 9, or `Scrolled{Id, Offset, Height}`, item 11) and returns `Handled{Signal, Changed}`: the signal for the program (`None` for a node with none, or one the tree no longer holds), and whether a cell changed, so the view must render again.
5. **View-local cells.** `NewDraft(f, init)` and `NewFlag(f, init)` read a cell keyed like a node: by the call's identity under the node that encloses it, so a keyed row keeps its own cells wherever it moves. `Field(f, draft, placeholder)` shows a draft (a typed text becomes its value and sends nothing), `Toggle(f, flag, label)` shows a flag (a press flips it), a list's viewport is a third kind of cell (item 11), and `Submit(f, draft, label, on) { … }` is a `Pressable` whose press also empties the draft. A cell a pass does not read is dropped, so a row that returns starts from `init`. The composer keeps only such host-owned widget state, as the DOM and Gio do; any other state is a field of the program's machine state. *Decided (corpus wave Q2, 2026-11-12):* the cells are a closed union (`TextCell`, `FlagCell`, `ViewCell`), not a store of arbitrary `T` per node, because Ergo has no erased value to keep many types in one tree, and a typed field of the machine state is the Elm and Lustre answer for the rest.
6. **Loads.** `Load(f, key, fetch)` asks for `fetch()`, a `func() E`, while its call returns with `key`. The pass never runs it: `Commit` decides the frame's `Jobs`, a `Fetch{Epoch, Run}` for a load that is new or has a new key or place, and a `Cancel{Epoch}` for the load it replaces and for a load a pass leaves out or whose node is removed. Every fetch has an epoch no other fetch of the frame had. `Arrive(f, epoch)` is whether the load of that epoch is still live: a host loop gives the program a fetch's signal only then, so a result never shows for a key it was not fetched for. The program keeps the result in its machine state as an `Async[T, F]` (`Loading`, `Ready{Value}`, `Failed{Error}`), matched exhaustively. *Decided (corpus wave H2b, 2026-11-12):* a result is a signal of the program, not a value the frame keeps, for Q2's reason. The fetch takes no capabilities: a fetch that waits captures what it waits on (a queue, a `time.Manual`).
7. **Sessions and Run.** A `Session` is the loop of one view over one actor, whatever the host. `Start(a, view, post)` renders the first pass; `Step(s, event)` handles one event, sends its signal to the actor and renders again, and returns the patch. Each `Fetch` of a pass runs as a task (`task.Go`) that calls `post(Arrived{Epoch, Signal})` with its result; each `Cancel` takes the fetch's task out of the session and stops it, so a cancelled fetch has ended when the pass returns, and its result, if it posted one, is dropped by `Arrive`. The session owns its tasks (a `Map[int, Task[bool]]` field, [resources.md](resources.md) R14), so dropping a session stops them. `Run(h, a, view)` drives a session on a `Host[E]` until the host fails `Closed`: it shows each patch, and the fetches post to `h.Post`, so a result reaches the loop through `h.Next`, on the loop's own thread, as input does (the host loop of the design model `models/uiloop`, whose laws `NoStaleReady`, `ShownCommitted` and `SettledShowsLatest` `ergo test models/uiloop` proves in a scope of five states). A host that drives a session itself, as the test host and the MyGo desktop driver (`hosts/mygo/desktop`) do, reads `SessionRoot(s)`, the root its mirror starts from, and `SessionAnimates(s)`, whether the last pass made an `Animate` and the host should send frames. **A state the loop did not cause** (DOMAIN PX3a): a reply its machine's work sends, or a timer's signal, reaches the actor with no event of the host's. The session's watcher task reads `a.LatestChanges()` (F-GP4c-1: a watcher its host blocks holds one state, not each commit; law `WatcherHoldsOneState`) and, at each state committed, marks the session stale and posts one `Committed` while it is not; a pass clears the mark and records the `actor.Version` of the state it read (read around the `State` read, exact unless a commit came between, and never later than the state), and the `Step` of a `Committed` renders again only when the actor's `Version` is past it, so a commit the loop's own signal made, whose pass shows it, costs no second pass (FK2-g2: a press is one pass; law `PressIsOnePass`, which counts the nodes a press writes at n and 4n rows against one pass of the view). The test host's `Settle` waits until the watcher, a task of the clock's domain, has posted the `Committed` of each state the work committed, and takes it, so after a `Settle` the screen shows a state the work committed; when every committed state was read by a pass, it adds no pass. A view that reads state outside the actor (a cell, a `sync.Ref`), whose change no event or commit announces, is drawn again by `Redraw(h)`. The laws `WorkReplyIsShown` (the test host) and `WatcherShowsWorkReplies` (a session as `Run` drives it, waiting on each post) are in `TestUIHostModel`. *Decided (corpus wave UC, 2026-11-13):* every state the actor commits redraws, as every `Msg` does in Elm's `update`/`view` loop and in Lustre's, by a watcher on the actor rather than by routing work's signals through the host. Reverse by a work `send` that posts to the host. A load's signal stays `comparable`, as every signal of a `Frame[E]` is (a node holds its signal, and nodes compare with `==`).
8. **The test host.** `NewTestHost(a, view, clock, room)` drives a session in process for a test or an agent; it shows a `Mirror` built only from the patches. `Tap(h, id)`, `TypeText(h, id, text)`, `Find(h, label)` (the first node on the screen, in tree order, whose label, text or placeholder is `label`), `Screen` and `Screenshot` (the screen as shapes, or as indented text), `StateOf`, `Fetching`, `Pump` (take the results already posted), `Settle` (wait until nothing is left to run at this instant: no fetch in flight, every result taken, and every task of the clock's domain waiting again, so a machine's work a signal started has run to its end or its next wait on the clock, with no Advance: `settledShowsLatest`, and the law `SettleRunsTheWork` in testhost.ergo, FK1-g6; *Decided (orchestrator, 2026-11-15):* settled is no pending work at this instant); a fetch asleep on the manual clock is left in flight: once every task of the domain waits and each fetch left is asleep on the clock (`time.Asleep`: it, or a task it started, sleeps there), `Settle` returns rather than wait for an `Advance` only the script makes, and `PlayTrace`'s line for that `SettleAll` ends `; waiting on the clock: N` (FK4-g2; law `CancelledFetchStops`: `Settle` never blocks on a sleeper the script could wake), and a fetch whose answer comes from outside the clock (a server's task) is waited for, also while a timer sleeps on the clock (FK4-g2-g1; law `OffClockAnswerIsWaitedFor`) and `Advance(h, d)`, which moves `clock`, the `time.Manual` the program's tasks sleep on; nothing else moves it. `PointAt(h, id, p)` and `PressKey(h, k)` send pointer and key events. `Play(h, script)` runs a scene script of `Act`s (`TapOn{Label}`, `TypeInto{Label, Text}`, `SettleAll`, `AdvanceBy{By}`, `KeyIn{Press}`, `PointOn{Label, Pointer}`), failing `NoNode` for a label it does not find. It lays nothing out: a tap names a node, not a point, until the layout kernel (`ui/layout`) gives boxes. *Decided (corpus wave H4, 2026-11-12):* the test host steps the same `Session` as `Run` instead of serving `Run` as a `Host`, so a test is synchronous and needs no second task, and what it shows is what `Run` shows for the same events.
9. **Pointer, keys and frames.** These events are data of the program's signal type too. `Area(f, label, style, on) { … }` is a container whose box takes pointer events: a `PointerEvent{Pointer, Phase, X, Y, Zone}` (`Pointer` is the mouse, finger or pen that did it, MB4's Fingers; `Phase` is `Down | Moved | Drag | Up | Wheel{DX, DY}`, the position in the box, and the zone under it, item 22), and `on` gives the signal, or `None` for an event the program ignores. `OnKey(f, on)` takes `KeyPress{Name, Mods}` (`Mods{Shift, Ctrl, Alt, Meta}`): a key goes to the first `OnKey` of the last pass that gives a signal, so a dialog written before its page takes its keys first. The keyboard's focus is the node last pressed or typed into (`Focused(f)`; UC-FOCUS). `OnFocusKey(f, on)`, written in a container, takes a key while the focus is on the container or inside it. A key goes first to the `OnFocusKey` of the focused node, then of each container above it, as a DOM `keydown` bubbles from its target, and only then to the `OnKey` calls. *Decided (UC-FOCUS, 2026-11-16):* focus follows the press, as a click focuses a control. Reverse by a focus the program holds in its model, set by its own signals. Tab and Shift+Tab, when no key function takes them, move the focus to the next or the previous `Focusable` node in tree order, round at the ends (UC-TAB): a `Pressable`, `Check`, `Toggle` or `Input`, or a `Semantic` a press sends from and that is not disabled, outside any disabled `Fieldset` (law `TabWalksTheFocusables`). A host reads and sets the focus with `SessionFocus` and `FocusSession` (`FocusOf(h)` on the test host); the desktop host draws its ring from it, so both hosts focus by one rule. `Animate(f, on)` asks for frames while a pass makes it. The host sends `Tick{At}` (the frame's time since the host started), and the session sends the signal `on(At)` of each `Animate` of the last pass, then renders once. A pass with no `Animate` asks for no frame (`Animating`): `Run` tells its host through `Host.Frames(on)` when that changes, and a settled screen gets no ticks. On the test host the agent owns the clock. `Advance(h, d)` moves it one frame period at a time (16ms, or the period set by `FrameEvery`) and steps a `Tick` at the end of each, while the view animates; after each `Tick` it takes the events already posted (`Pump`), as the live loop takes its queue after each frame, so an animating session never leaves its watcher blocked on a full queue (F-GP4c-1; law `AdvanceTakesThePosted`). Time short of a period is owed to the next `Advance`. `WantsFrames` and `Frames` report the frame state.
10. **Canvas.** `Canvas(f, label, width, height) func(p *ui.Paint) { … }` is a leaf whose content is a paint list of values (`PaintOp`): `FillOp{Path, Color}`, `StrokeOp{Path, Color, Width}`, and `PushTransform{M}` or `PushClip{X, Y, W, H}`, each closed by a `PopPaint`. A `Path` is a list of `Seg`s (`MoveTo`, `LineTo`, `QuadTo`, `CubicTo`, `ClosePath`; `RectPath` builds a rectangle), a `Color` has four bytes, and an `Affine` uses the canvas `setTransform` order (`Identity`, `Translate`, `Scale`). The draw function paints through `Fill`, `Stroke`, `Transformed(p, m) { … }` and `Clipped(p, x, y, w, h) { … }`; the last two write their push and pop around the body, so a list is always balanced. Only `Canvas` makes a `Paint`, and it lives for one call of the draw function. **Lowering (CANVAS-PATHS):** every canvas of a frame paints into the frame's one `Paint`, a scratch kept from canvas to canvas and pass to pass: `Fill` and `Stroke` copy their path's segments into it, and each op notes where its path ends. When the draw function returns, `Canvas` copies out what it added, as one list of ops and one list of segments that the paths are windows of, and cuts the scratch back. So a canvas costs two allocations, whatever it paints and however its list grew, and a path built for one call (`RectPath`) is copied in and never kept, so it needs no heap. A canvas written inside another's draw adds after the other's ops and is cut back before the other goes on, so each list is what its own draw painted (`PaintMatchesList`). The value rule needs the copy: a window of the scratch kept by a node would be copied there anyway, since the scratch is written again. The list is a field of `CanvasNode`, and a `Node` compares structurally ([types.md, Operators](types.md#operators) 1), so a pass that paints the same list changes nothing, and a new list is one `Set`. `PaintOf(node)` reads it back, and the test host's `Screenshot` prints it under its canvas, one op a line. *Decided (corpus wave H5c, 2026-11-12):* `Paint` is an explicit borrow, as the frame is (item 1, Q1), and not a capability: it holds no type parameter today, but a borrow keeps one rule for every surface a view writes to.

   *Images (G9 UI-IMAGE, 2026-11-19).* `DrawImage(p, src, x, y, w, h)` draws the image a host finds at `src` stretched into the rectangle at `x, y` of `w` by `h`, and `DrawSprite(p, src, region, x, y, w, h)` draws the `ImageRegion{X, Y, W, H}` of it, in the image's pixels (a sprite of an atlas): both write `ImageOp{Src, Part, X, Y, W, H}`, whose `Part` is `WholeImage` or the region, under the transforms and clips around it. The paint list holds the source's name, never its pixels, so a list stays data a test compares; the test host's screenshot prints `image SRC [part X Y W H] at X Y W H`. The desktop host (`screen.Window.Image`) decodes a PNG or JPEG file at `src` once, keeps it by name, and draws it under the canvas's transform; a file it cannot read draws nothing. `PaintMatchesList` draws images and sprites among its ops, and `TestImageDrawsItsPart` (hosts/mygo/screen) checks the window's pixels: the region, not the whole atlas, under the transform, and nothing for a missing file. *Decided:* the host decodes, not std, so the paint list stays comparable data and a host that has no file system can resolve names its own way. Reverse by an image value in the op. Not yet: image tinting, pixelated sampling, a size a view can read, and hostagree probes of an image's pixels.
11. **Virtual rows.** `Rows(f, label, count, rowHeight, key) func(i int) { … }` writes a scroll container (`RowsNode{Label, Count, RowHeight, First}`) of `count` rows, each `rowHeight` high. The composer runs the row function only for the rows that `Visible(count, rowHeight, viewport)` gives: the rows the viewport shows, plus `Overscan` (2) rows on each side. A host places them at `First * rowHeight` in content `count * rowHeight` high. The viewport (`Viewport{Offset, Height}`) is view-local state the host owns, a `ViewCell` keyed like any cell. A host reports it with `Scrolled{Id, Offset, Height}` as it scrolls or sizes the list, and the next pass writes the rows it shows. A `Scrolled` at which `Visible` gives the rows it gave before runs no pass (SCROLL-COST): the list keeps its rows and takes the new `Offset` in one `Set`, which a host applies as a pass's patch; the law `ScrollCostsNoViewPass` (in `TestUIHostModel`) checks that such a scroll's step counts no pass and that every scroll's screen is the list cut at its viewport. A scroll that brings in a row still runs the view. It is (0, 0) until a host reports one. A row's identity and cells are by `key(i)`, as in `Each`, so a row keeps its toggles and drafts while it stays among the rows written, and starts from `init` when it comes back. The test host's `ScrollTo(h, id, offset, height)` (`ScrollOn{Label, Offset, Height}`) reports a viewport. `RowRuns(h)` counts the row functions the last pass ran: the work grows with the viewport, not with the count. *Decided (corpus wave H5b, 2026-11-12):* the row height is fixed and given, and the viewport comes from the host as an event, so the list needs no layout kernel. Rows of measured heights come with `ui/layout` (H2L). The node carries the offset the pass used (`Offset`), so a host scrolls to it when it is not its own. `RowsKeeping(f, label, count, rowHeight, keep, key) func(i int) { … }` is a list that follows a cursor (GP1b): when `keep` changed since the last pass and is a row, the offset moves the least that shows the whole row (`Keep(keep, rowHeight, v)`: the row's top when it starts above the viewport or the viewport is shorter than a row, its bottom less the height when it ends below); a scroll of the host's after it stays while `keep` stays, so a user can scroll away from the cursor. The law `RowsKeepTheCursor` (in `TestUIHostModel`) checks the offset and the rows shown against a model under drawn scrolls and cursor moves. *Decided (corpus wave UC, 2026-11-13):* the list keeps a row in view as Compose's `LazyListState.scrollToItem` and Flutter's `Scrollable.ensureVisible` do, by a value the view passes each pass, not by a command; the last `keep` is part of the list's view state, so only a change moves it. Reverse by a command event the program sends.
12. **Text layout and the clipboard.** `Measure` is the operation type of the host's text measurement: `Width(text, font)` and `LineHeight(font)`, with the laws `EmptyIsZero` and `WidthGrows` (text followed by more text is never narrower, which line breaking needs). `Monospace{Advance, Line}` is the measure of a cell grid and of tests. `LayoutText(m, text, font, width)` breaks text into `Line{Start, End, Width, Wrapped}` (byte offsets at rune boundaries). It breaks at each newline, and otherwise after the last run of spaces that keeps the line within `width`. The spaces stay at the end of the line and take no width. A word wider than `width` breaks between runes, with at least one rune per line. `CaretAt(m, l, offset)` gives the caret's `X`, `Top` and `Height`; the end of a wrapped line is the start of the next. `OffsetAt(m, l, x, y)` is the rune boundary whose caret is nearest the point. `SelectionRects(m, l, from, to)` gives one `Rect` per line a selection holds runes of. `Clipboard` is a capability (`Read`, `Write`, law `WriteThenRead`) that `cmd/<name>/main.ergo` binds; `MemoryClipboard` is its test provider. An editor program keeps its buffer, caret and undo in its own machine state and asks these functions where things are. *Decided (corpus wave H5d, 2026-11-12):* layout takes the measure as a value, not a `needs`, so a view, a machine step and a host can all call it. The greedy break is UAX #14 reduced to spaces and newlines; the full line-break classes, bidi text and grapheme clusters belong to the host's text engine behind `Measure`.
13. **Lowering and skipping.** The composer, the arena and the patch are Ergo bodies: the tree is an `arena.Of` of nodes holding Refs, not pointers. With no component call and no `Same`, every pass writes its whole view and `Commit` compares every node. A pass reuses the last pass's storage: its specs, cells, loads and key and frame functions are the front of lists kept across passes, its identity counts carry the pass's number, so the next pass starts with none and deletes none, its index of the last pass's children (one flat `Map` keyed by the parent and the identity) is built only for a parent whose old child at a node's place is not that node (a pass whose children come back in order looks the old child up at its place; `Unplaced(f)` counts the lookups that miss) and emptied in place, and its tree is linked through the specs, so it builds no child list. The composer reads one field of a retained node in place, through `arena.Update`'s borrow, and writes cells and loads only into the nodes that hold or get some. A parent whose children are the old children in their places is reconciled with no list at all. A steady pass of a 200-row view (601 nodes) makes about 15 allocations in the composer; the rest of its 1,019 are the view's own (closures, texts, and each `Node` a primitive boxes into its union) (GP frame cost, UC-1 to UC-3). A *component call* is a statement call of a view function the program declares, `card(f, it)`, whose first argument is the frame and whose other arguments are values of types that compare with `==` and hold no pointer, written as any expression with no failure or capability row (no `try`, `catch` or call with a row), the call itself having none. A computed argument (`row(f, x, "row \(x)")`, `len(xs)`, a pure call) is evaluated once, in order, into a local that the group compares and the call reads, as an ordinary call evaluates it once (NS2a); a name, a field selection or a constant is read in both places. An argument of a record type is compared by the fields the component reads (NS2b): the checker records, for each function with a body, the top-level fields its body reads of each record parameter (`Info.Reads`; `p.f.g` reads `f`, passing `p` whole to a function of the program reads what that function reads, recursion joins to the least fixpoint, and any other use, a spread, a match, a dot call, a call of a function whose body it cannot see, reads all of it). When the component does not read all of its record and each field it reads compares with `==` and holds no pointer, the record is evaluated once into a local and the group compares those fields alone, so a record that holds a slice is a group when the component reads no slice of it, and a change to a field it does not read writes it again. `SetUIReadsForTest(false)` selects the whole-argument rule. A compared value (an argument, or a read field) may also be a slice, a `Map`, or a record, tuple or union that holds one, when it reaches no pointer and no function (NS2c): the call then goes through the composer's `groupBy`, which compares the value by its generated structural equality (the `==` the program's own comparison lowers to) against a copy of its own that the group keeps, made only when the call runs, so a later write in place through the program's value (a `let mut` of a parameter that is dead after it) never reaches what the next pass compares. It makes that copy only on the group's first pass and after a pass whose values `==` compares (its cheap values: the scalars, records and unions among them) were equal to the last ones; after a pass where they changed, as in each frame of an animation whose time is an argument, it keeps the arguments as they are and the next pass compares only the cheap values and runs the group, so no shared slice is read and a group that changes every pass pays no copy. A view that stops changing runs such a group once more, then skips it (`GroupByKeepsAfterCheapEqual`; P-ui-flow). Such a field has no constant-time equality, since a value copy keeps no identity to compare, so the compare is O(n) in the value against the O(nodes) run it may skip (`SetUIEqForTest(false)` selects the NS2b rule, where such a call is no group). The compiler passes it through the composer's `writeGroup`, keyed like a node (the call's site and `Each` row under the enclosing node, counted apart from the nodes): when the call returns under the same node with arguments equal to the last pass's, `writeGroup` writes the top nodes the last pass's call wrote, each skipped as a skipped `Same` is (item 18), without running it: what is below them is the last pass's, so it is neither written, compared nor stored. That is sound because a view is pure apart from its writes through `f` (item 1): equal arguments write equal nodes. A call that read a cell, a load, a key, an address or a frame callback, skipped a `Same` or stopped a group is never kept, nor one whose nodes would take other identities (a sibling at the same site came or went), nor one whose nodes left the host's tree or changed their disabled state; it runs every pass. *The group protocol* (SP9) is any library's, not std ui's alone: a package's generic frame type `T[E]` has a group when the package declares a function of the shape `func g[E, A](f *T[E], args A, body func(), at CallSite)` and a top-level law that reaches `g` (names it, or names a function of the package that does): the law the skip rests on, which the package's `ergo test` runs; std ui's is `GroupSkipsEqualArguments`. A component call is a call of a view on any such frame from outside the frame's package; a call of the frame package's own functions (its primitives, such as `ui.Text`) is never one. The compiler finds the pair by that shape and that law, never by a name or by std membership, so a user's UI library gets the Go std ui gets (`TestGroupProtocolCopyAgrees`, over std ui copied into a user package, and the same copy without the law, whose calls stay plain); a group no law reaches is an ordinary function. *Decided (SP9, 2026-11-16):* the law's presence in the package is the declared contract, not a ledger row of evidence for it; reverse by also requiring its proved or validated row in the declaring module's ledger, as a cell provider's laws are admitted (`evidenceFor`). groupBy takes the same rule: the compiler sends a call through a frame package's groupBy only under a top-level law that reaches it, and a call whose compared values need generated equality is no component call when the package has none. The arguments are held by the runtime (`ui.GroupArgs`, a slot per kept call, freed when the call stops returning), since Ergo has no type for a value of any comparable type. The kept groups stay in place from pass to pass: a pass writes the entry of each group that ran, marks the entries of the groups it wrote again (and of the groups inside them), and frees the rest, so a pass that keeps every group writes no entry and allocates nothing for them; it counts the held entries it marks, so such a pass walks no entry either (`Swept(f)` counts the entries the last pass walked; NS2c fix 2). A pass starts each of the frame's maps the last pass wrote afresh rather than deleting its keys, and counts the groups' identities by the pass's number, as the nodes'. `GroupRuns(f)` counts the component calls the last pass ran, `GroupsKept(f)` the calls kept for the next. The reference lowering (`SetUISkipForTest(false)`, or a compiler built with `rewriteLowering=reference`) runs every call; `TestUISkipAgrees` holds both to the same trees, patches and signals over generated components, states and presses, and one changed card of 8 or 64 to one run. *Decided (P-ui, 2026-11-13):* the compiler finds components from the types, as Compose's compiler skips a composable whose stable arguments are equal, rather than an author's marker (Elm's `Html.Lazy`, React's `memo`); the narrow operand rule (no call in an argument) kept the arguments' second evaluation free. *Decided (NS2a, 2026-11-15, measured):* any argument without a row, evaluated once into a local, as Compose's compiler compares any argument expression's value in the callee's prologue; the bench's `row(f, x, "row \(x)")` form was never kept before (its pass cost what the reference's does). When every row's argument changes, each row runs once and `Commit` compares the nodes the reference compares (`allChange` in `TestUISkipAgrees`). *Decided (NS2b, 2026-11-15, after lane CK's probe, `nsh/ns2b-probe.md`):* compare the read fields only when each compares in constant time, as Compose skips only on stable parameters and turns skipping off for an unstable `List`; in the corpus 11 of 22 components are groups by this rule against 7 by the whole-argument one (task-board's `cardView` among them). A list holder (`list(f, s)` reading `s.rows`) is not reached by this rule; NS2c reaches it. *Decided (NS2c, 2026-11-15, measured; `corpus-wave/LY/ns2c-proposal.md` and `ns2c-receipt.md`):* a list holder is compared structurally against a deep copy kept by the group (option a), not by identity (which needs a whole-program alias fact) and not through a persistent vector in std (a language change), as Compose compares a stable collection with its `equals`. A hand-written version measured about 10 instructions a row of ints for the compare; the change case costs the copy. *Decided (P-ui templates, 2026-11-13, measured):* no templates (a view's static nodes written once). On a list of 800 rows with 15 static nodes of 3,217, `Commit` takes 69% of a pass (reconcile 23%, keeping groups 18%, child lists 15%) and the view 27%; the static nodes are 0.5% of the nodes, so templates could save at most that. The lever the profile names is a `Commit` that skips the subtree of a group written again, whose nodes it knows are unchanged, and `Commit` does so: a top node a kept group writes again is matched among its siblings (and moved if it moved) but neither it nor its subtree is compared, and the group is kept as it was. `Compared(f)` counts the nodes the last `Commit` compared or made; one changed card of 8 or 64 compares its own three. Culling (no node written off screen) is a later unit. Each top node a kept group writes again takes its identity count once, checked as it is taken; a group refused partway gives back the counts its tops took (NS2c fix 1). A group whose tops each took the first count of their identity and share one row site, written again where no node before it under the parent (at most four, walked) has that row site, takes no count at all until a later write under the parent asks for one; a cut gives back the counts of such groups it takes back (NS2c fix 4). A kept group written again is one spec, a block standing for all its top nodes: `Commit` finds the block in place when its first and last tops are at their places (a kept group's tops are contiguous and in order among their parent's children, since the pass that kept them committed that parent and none has since), and expands it to its tops only for a parent whose children moved, as `FrameShape` and `PassStats` do; its tops' disabled state is checked by two facts kept with the group, and the groups inside it stay kept through it rather than each being marked (NS2c fix 5). *Measured (NS1, 2026-11-15; `corpus-wave/LY/ns1-receipt.md`):* in the rows bench (a column, a tick text, N rows of a row box, a text and a pressable), a pass that changes only the tick costs, with the collector off, 1.77 million instructions and 21 allocations at 200 rows against the reference lowering's 6.26 million and 1,010, and 7.0 million at 800; no row runs and `Commit` compares the four nodes outside them (the guard `elsewhere` in `TestUISkipAgrees`, at 50 and 200 rows). The residue, about 8,800 instructions a row, is `Each`'s walk and each row's group: its identity count, the key's lookup, the arguments' compare and the top node's spec, most of it hashing the call sites' file names in the frame's maps; skipping a list whose rows are all kept is NS2b.
14. **Laws.** `IncrementalEqualsFresh` (generated view programs over generated states: after each pass of a run, the frame, the tree, the mirror built from the patches and a fresh frame's mirror all show what the program means at that state, computed without a frame, and the tree holds no node more), `IdempotentCommit` (a second pass of an equal view is an empty patch), `KeyedIdentity` (a permutation of keyed rows keeps every row's Ids, and the node after the `Each`, with only `Move`s) and `PressGivesSignal` run in `TestUITreeModel`. `StateKeeps` and `FreshMountInits` (drawn passes, texts and presses against a model of each row's cells: the screen, `Changed` and a `Submit`'s signal agree with it after each step, with rows reordered, and with rows also left out and returned) and `LoadFollowsModel` (the model of `models/uiloop`: drawn passes and finishes in any order give exactly the jobs the model wants, `Arrive` and `Handle` of an `Arrived` take a result exactly for the live epoch, and once every fetch has finished the program holds the live load's result) run in `TestUILoadModel`. `ScriptShowsTheView` (drawn scripts of presses by label on a keyed list over a machine actor: after each step the test host's screen is a fresh pass over the actor's state, that state is the model's, and the clock has not moved) and `CancelledFetchStops` (a fetch that says when it starts and ends and sleeps on the clock: when a press that changes the key returns, the old fetch has ended and one is in flight; after `Advance` and `Settle` the live key's result shows and nothing is in flight), `NoTickWhenSettled` (drawn presses that ask for 0 to 3 frames and clock moves: the ticks, their times, `WantsFrames` and the screen agree with a model of the frame clock, so no tick is sent while the view does not animate) and `InputGivesSignal` (drawn pointer events on an `Area` whose function reads the state, and keys with and without Ctrl under a dialog's `OnKey`: the actor's state is the model's fold of the signals) and `PaintMatchesList` (a drawn paint program of fills, strokes, rectangles, transforms and clips, nested, over states a press moves to: the canvas on the test host holds exactly the list the program means, written without a `Paint`, the screenshot has a line per node and per op, and a second pass at the same state is an empty patch) and `RowsAreTheCutEach` (a keyed list of up to 1,000 rows with a toggle each, under drawn scrolls, resizes, adds, drops of the first row and toggles: the list on the test host is the full list cut to the rows a brute-force model of the viewport writes, each toggle as the model keeps it by key, and `RowRuns` is the number of rows written, bounded by the viewport) run in `TestUIHostModel`. `LinesCoverText` (drawn texts of words, spaces, newlines, overlong words and multi-byte runes at drawn widths and advances: the lines cover the text in order, hold no newline, are as wide as their text without trailing spaces, overflow only with one rune, and are greedy), `HitFindsTheCaret` (the offset under the middle of each boundary's caret is that boundary, and a drawn selection's boxes hold each rune's middle exactly when it is selected), `Measure.EmptyIsZero`, `Measure.WidthGrows` and `Clipboard.WriteThenRead` run in `TestUITextModel`.
15. **Boundaries.** A read whose data has not arrived fails with `Pending`, an error type in the view's `fails` row like any other failure, so a signature shows what a view can stop on. `Boundary(f) { … } catch { case Pending: … case users.NotFound: … }` is a group with no node of its own, and every primitive that takes a body (`Box`, `Row`, `Column`, `Scroll`, `Pressable`, `Submit`, `Area`, `Each`, `Rows`) is a group too: its body's row is a row parameter `F` of the primitive, so a read can sit in any block, and `try` passes the failure on. When a failure leaves a group's body, the group cuts the pass back to where it started: no node, key or frame function, identity count or cell that the body wrote stays. What a boundary's body writes directly under the parent is identified under the boundary's call site, as an `Each`'s rows are under the `Each`'s, so a sibling after the boundary keeps its identity however far the body got; two calls of one boundary site under one parent in one row share their counts, so a component that holds a boundary and is called twice in one parent is written inside a container or an `Each` row. A catch arm writes at the caller's sites: a helper called both in an arm and after the group shares its count with the arm (item 3). Then the failure goes to the caller's `catch`, whose arms write at the group's place, or to the next group out. A stopped body makes no node and no patch op, and the siblings before and after the group keep their nodes and cells. What the group held in earlier passes is **parked**, not dropped: an old child that a parent with a stopped group under it does not write leaves the tree (the host gets its `Remove`) but keeps its `Id`, cells, loads and subtree, and comes back with the same `Id` when a later pass writes it. The cells and loads read directly under that parent are kept too, so a draft, a flag or a viewport under a boundary keeps its value through any number of pending or failed passes. A parked load is not cancelled: its fetch runs and `Arrive` holds for it. Parked nodes are released (cells dropped, loads cancelled) at the first pass in which no group under their parent stops. `Len` counts the tree and `Retained` every node the frame holds, parked ones included. A forgotten boundary is a compile error that names the types: `Render`, `Run`, `Start` and `NewTestHost` take a view with an empty row, so a read in an exported view is S101 at the read (`{NotFound | ui.Pending} escapes function Spelled, whose error row {} does not list it`), a read in a container's block is S101 at the container's call, and a view whose row was inferred is S108 at the call of `Render` (`its error row {NotFound | ui.Pending} is wider than {}`). A failure is not a defect: a panic passes every boundary (rows.md, Catch 6). *Hazard:* a plain `catch` around a call of a program's own function that wrote nodes and then failed, with no group around it, leaves those nodes; put the call in `Boundary` (the lint `ui/catch-writes` comes with the lint SDK). The design model `models/uiboundary` (`ergo test models/uiboundary`) and the law `BoundaryFollowsModel` in `TestUILoadModel` (drawn passes with a load and drafts beside a boundary and inside it, a Row, keyed rows and a load inside the body, failing at drawn places, against a model of the drafts and the live loads) hold the rule; `IncrementalEqualsFresh` and `IdempotentCommit` generate views with nested boundaries whose reads fail by the state. *Decided (corpus wave H8a, 2026-11-13):* parking is per parent, not per group: a parent with any stopped group under it keeps all its unwritten children and unread cells and loads, until a pass in which no group under it stops. Reverse by cells tagged with their group. A load that a stopped body writes under a node with no old node to park in is fetched under the group's parent, so a body that loads and then reads does not wait for ever; the first completed pass then fetches it again once under its own node (a known cost).
16. **Locators and matchers.** A scene finds nodes as Testing Library's queries do. Each node has an `Access`: a `Role` from its kind alone (`Button` for a `Pressable` or `Submit`, `Checkbox`, `Switch` for a `Toggle`, `TextBox` for an `Input` or `Field`, `Static` for a `Text`, `Group` for a `Fieldset`, `Region` for an `Area`, `Image` for a `Canvas`, `List` for `Rows`, and `Generic` for a layout container), an accessible name (the label the primitive took, a `Text`'s value, an `Input`'s placeholder), `Disabled`, `Description`, `Checked` and `Value`. `Fieldset(f, disabled, description) { … }` is the container that carries the last two, as HTML's `fieldset` and `aria-describedby` do: a control (`Button`, `Checkbox`, `Switch`, `TextBox`) under a disabled `Fieldset` is disabled, so `Handle` does nothing on a press or a text on it, and a node's description is that of the nearest `Fieldset` (itself included) that has one. A `Locator` is `WithRole{Role, Name}` (an empty name matches any), `WithLabel{Label}`, `WithPlaceholder{Text}` or `WithText{Text}`, and names match exactly. `LocateIn(m, loc)` gives the nodes of a mirror it finds, in tree order; the root is the host's and never found. `AccessOf(m, id)` reads one node and `TextIn(m, id)` its text content (the values of the `Text`s in its subtree). A `Matcher` is `ToExist`, `ToBeAbsent`, `ToHaveCount{Count}`, and, of the first match, `ToBeEnabled`, `ToBeDisabled`, `ToBeChecked`, `ToBeUnchecked`, `ToHaveValue{Value}`, `ToContainText{Text}` or `ToHaveDescription{Text}`: Playwright's names. `ExpectIn(m, loc, is)` fails `Mismatch{Want, Got}`. On the test host, `Locate`, `AccessAt` and `Expect` read its screen, and a script's `ClickOn{At}` and `FillIn{At, Text}` act on the one node a locator finds, failing `NoNode` when it finds none and `Ambiguous{At, Count}` when it finds more than one (NS7; `First{At}` finds the first of them), and `ExpectOn{At, Is}` checks a matcher. The law `LocatorAgreesWithAccessTree` (in `TestUITreeModel`) checks every role, and a drawn node's role and name, label, placeholder and text, against an accessibility tree computed from the model's shapes, with every matcher on the first match; `PressGivesSignal` checks that nothing under a disabled `Fieldset` sends a signal, and `ScriptShowsTheView` presses by locator and expects. *Decided (corpus wave H4r, 2026-11-13):* disabled and described are one container kind, not fields of every control, so the `Node` variants of the controls are unchanged. Reverse by fields on the controls.

**The screen as data** (TREE-JSON, RUNE §6). An agent reads a headless run without Go code. `TreeOf(m, boxes)` is a mirror's nodes that are not `Generic`, in tree order, each a `TreeEntry{Role, Name, States, Box}`: its role as `RoleName` writes it, its accessible name, its states (`StatesOf`: `disabled`, `checked` or `unchecked`, `selected`, `expanded` or `collapsed`, `pressed`, `invalid`, `busy`, `modal`, `value=V`, ARIA's names) and the box the host gave it (`None` with none). `TreeLine(e)` writes one as a JSON object, `{"role":…,"name":…,"states":[…],"box":[x,y,w,h]}`. A script can be data too: `ParseActs(text)` reads a JSON list of acts, each an object of one member, `{"tap": LABEL}`, `{"type": [LABEL, TEXT]}`, `{"key": NAME}`, `{"settle": true}`, `{"advance": MILLISECONDS}` or `{"scroll": [LABEL, OFFSET, HEIGHT]}`, and fails `BadScript{Why}` naming the act. MyGo's desktop host gives `TreeLines(d)`, the tree with its last paint's boxes, and the window binaries take `-headless -script FILE [-tree] [-png PICTURE]`: they play the act list, print the screen after it, one JSON line a node, and write its picture (`screen.Window.WritePNG`, the headless window's last frame). The laws (in tree.ergo, beside `TreeOf` and `ParseActs`; run by `TestUITreeModel`): `TreeAgreesWithLocate` (over the screens `LocatorAgreesWithAccessTree` draws, the tree is the model screen's nodes that are not `Generic`, each the node `WithRole` finds with its access, its ARIA states and the box the host gave it) and `ActsReadAsWritten` (a drawn act list written as JSON reads back as the same acts). `TestHeadlessScriptIsData` (hosts/mygo) plays a seeded counter-gui script as JSON and on standard input, requires the same lines, reads every tree line as JSON with a laid-out box, and finds the inc button drawn in the PNG. *Decided (TREE-JSON, 2026-11-19):* the tree is std ui's, so every host prints the same entries and only the boxes and the picture are the host's. Reverse by a host-side tree.

**Strict acts** (NS7). An act fails `Ambiguous{At, Count}` when what it names finds more than one node, as Playwright's strict locators do; it sends nothing. This covers `ClickOn` and `FillIn` by locator, and `TapOn`, `TypeInto`, `PointOn` and `ScrollOn` by label. A label's matches are counted as `Find` matches them, a match inside a match being the same target (a `Pressable "go"` around a `Text "go"` is one node to press). The locator `First{At}` finds the first node `At` finds, in tree order, so a script names the one it means. `Find` still gives the first: it is a query, not an act. The locator `Within{In, At}` finds the nodes `At` finds that are under a node `In` finds, at any depth and not `In`'s own, in tree order (FK11-g6), as Testing Library's `within(container)` and Playwright's `locator.locator(…)`: a scene names a column by its region, then a card in it, and `First` and the strict acts compose with it. The law `WithinFindsUnder` (in locate.ergo) draws boards of columns whose card labels repeat within and across columns, some cards a level down, and requires each column's cards in order, none for a missing column, no column within a column, a nested `Within` to agree, and a click of `First` within a column to press that column's card. The law `AmbiguousActFails` draws screens with 2 to 5 buttons and inputs that share a name among unique ones. Each act on them must fail with that count and send nothing, `First` must reach the first, and `Find` must agree with `First`. `ScriptShowsTheView` now presses the first row's "del" through `First`. *Decided (corpus wave NS7, 2026-11-15):* strict acts, replacing H4r's first match. Reverse by an act that takes the first, which `First` already gives by name.

**The semantics record** (NS4). `Access` also has `Selected`, `Expanded` and `Pressed` (each `Option[bool]`: None for a node without that state), `Invalid`, `Busy`, `HasPopup` and `Level` (`Option[int]`, a heading's). `Role` also has the kit's roles, `Tab`, `TabList`, `TabPanel`, `Dialog`, `Menu`, `MenuItem`, `Tree`, `TreeItem`, `Heading`, `Alert`, `Link`, `Table`, `TableRow` (`Row` is a container) and `TableCell`, after WAI-ARIA's. A built-in control's record still comes from its kind, with the new fields empty. `Semantic(f, access, on, style) { … }` is a container (`SemanticNode{Access, On, Style}`) that declares its whole record. It is disabled also under a disabled Fieldset, and has the Fieldset's description when it has none. `WithLabel` finds it by its name, and a press on it sends `on`, when it has one and is not disabled. The matchers `ToBeSelected`, `ToBeExpanded`, `ToBeCollapsed` (a node that expands and is not expanded) and `ToBeInvalid` read the new states. The law `AccessIsWhatWasDeclared`, in locate.ergo, draws records of every role and state, some under a disabled Fieldset, and requires `AccessAt` and the four matchers to give each record back. A press must send only from an enabled node, and the records a press redraws (Set ops) must read back too. *Decided (corpus wave NS4, 2026-11-15):* a kit control declares its semantics on one container, not through new fields on each built-in control, so the built-in `Node` variants are unchanged and a new state is one field. The container takes the press, so a tab is one node, as ARIA puts the role on the element that is pressed. Reverse by fields on `PressableNode`. The cell host maps every role and state ([UI cell host](#ui-cell-host) 2); the accessibility-tree host (H3g) maps them when it lands.

**Roles, states and relations** (UG2). `Role` also has `Slider`, `SpinButton`, `SearchBox`, `MenuItemCheckbox`, `ComboBox`, `Grid`, `GridCell`, `ColumnHeader`, `RowHeader`, `Navigation`, `ListItem`, `Separator` and `Tooltip`, and `RoleName(r)` is a role's WAI-ARIA name. `Access` also has `Mixed` (a checkbox neither checked nor not, `aria-checked="mixed"`), `Current` (`Option[string]`: `"page"`, `"step"` or `"true"`, as `aria-current`), `Modal`, `MultiSelectable`, `Range` (`Option[Range{Now, Min, Max}]`, a slider's or spin button's value), `Key` (the node's own key, `NoKey` for none) and `Rels`, its relations: a `Rel` is `Controls{To}`, `LabelledBy{To}`, `DescribedBy{To}` or `ActiveDescendant{To}`, each naming a `Key`, or `OpensPopup{Role}`. A relation names a node by the key its `Access` declares, not by an `Id`, so a view writes a tab's `Controls` before the panel it names, as HTML writes an id reference. `RelationsIn(m, id)` (`RelationsAt(h, id)` on the test host) gives each relation with the node it names: the first node of the screen, in tree order, that declares the key, or `None` when none does. The law `RelationsNameHeldNodes` (in relate.ergo) draws screens of nodes, some keyed, with relations to keys some node declares and some none does, and requires each relation to resolve to the node that declares its key, and to nothing once a pass leaves that node out. The MyGo desktop gives each `Semantic` node its role, states, level, range, current item, popup, description and its `Controls` and `DescribedBy` relations in the window's accessibility tree (`hosts/mygo/desktop/semantics.ergo`), and hostagree's `roles` app checks the tree against what ui reads, node by node. MyGo's tree has no field for `LabelledBy`, `ActiveDescendant`, `Modal` or `Pressed`: those stay ui's. *Decided (UG2, 2026-11-17):* a relation names a key, not an `Id`, because a view cannot know an `Id` before its pass, and a key is what `Each` and a zone already name a node by. Reverse by an `Id` a host gives back.

**Labelled fields and choices** (the Access unit: FK3-g5, FK5-g3, FK2-g1, FK10-g2). `LabelledInput(f, FieldLabel{Text, Description, Invalid}, value, placeholder, onText)` is an `Input` that says what it is, as HTML's `label`, `aria-describedby` and `aria-invalid` do: `InputNode` has `Label`, `Description` and `Invalid` (empty for a plain `Input` or `Field`). Its accessible name is its label, and its placeholder only when it has none; its description is its own, else the nearest Fieldset's; `ToBeInvalid` reads its `Invalid`. `WithLabel` finds it by its label, and a script's `TypeInto{Label}` names it by its name, as `Find` does. The test host's screenshot and the desktop host draw the three: the label above the field, the description under it, an invalid field's border in the danger color. `Named(role, name)` is a node's record with no state, so a `Semantic` declaration is one call and sets only the states its control has (a heading's `Level` on the copy). `Radios(f, label, options, selected, on)` and `Select(f, label, options, selected, on)` pick one value among distinct options. Each is a `Semantic` of role `RadioGroup` or `ListBox`, named `label`, whose `Value` is the selected option, as a screen reader announces it. It holds one `Semantic` per option, of role `Radio` (with `Checked`) or `ListOption` (with `Selected`; ARIA's `option`), named by the option and pressed to pick it. Keys pick as ARIA's patterns do, while the focus is in the choice (`OnFocusKey`), so of two choices on one screen the keys move the one last pressed (law `ChoicesTakeKeysWhenFocused`, in choice.ergo): `ArrowDown` and `ArrowUp` move one option, `Home` and `End` go to the ends; a radio group also takes `ArrowRight` and `ArrowLeft` and wraps round, and a list box stops at its ends. `LabelledField(f, label, draft, placeholder)` is the same for a `Field`; todo-gui's field is one, so the desktop agreement tests draw a label and a description. The law `TypeIntoNamesTheField`, in testhost.ergo, draws screens of labelled and unlabelled fields and requires `TypeInto` to type into the field its name names, and a labelled field's placeholder to name no node. The law `LocatorAgreesWithAccessTree` now draws a labelled `Input` (with or without a label and a description of its own, invalid or not) and finds it by label and placeholder every pass. The law `ChoiceIsAnnouncedAndReachable`, in choice.ergo, draws radio groups and list boxes of 3 to 6 options, a selection and a target. It requires the announcement (the group's `Value`, and exactly one option checked or selected, found by role and by label), `Home` and `End`, every option reached one per arrow press both ways, the wrap or the stop at either end, the target reached, no signal from another key, and a press on an option to pick it. *Decided (corpus wave, the Access unit, 2026-11-16):* the label is three fields of `InputNode` and one more primitive, not a `Fieldset` around the input, because a label belongs to one control and `aria-describedby` names one. A choice is two primitives over `Semantic`, not new `Node` variants, so a host that draws `Semantic` draws them. Reverse by a `Label` container. Known gap: `ui` has no keyboard focus, so a choice's keys go to the first `OnKey` that takes them, and with two choices on a screen the first one takes the arrows.
17. **Location.** A `URL` is a place in a program: `Path`, `Query` and `Fragment`, as a browser's location gives them without its origin; `Href` prints it as `/path?query#fragment` and `ParseURL` reads it back (law `HrefParses`). `Location` is the operation type of a program's history of places: `Current`, `Push(u)` (an entry after the current one, dropping those after it), `Replace(u)` (the current entry's place) and `Back()` (false at the first entry), with the laws `PushThenBackRestores`, `ReplaceKeepsDepth` and `BackStopsAtTheStart`. The program's work holds it, and `cmd/<name>/main.ergo` binds it: `MemoryHistory` (its copies share one history) on the desktop and in a test, the page's history on the web. A change the program did not make (the user's back, a typed address) reaches the loop as the event `URLChanged{URL}`, and goes to the `OnURL` calls of the last pass in pass order, the first that gives a signal taking it, as `OnKey` does; a `Push` or `Replace` sends none, as `pushState` fires no `popstate`. The test host holds a `MemoryHistory` at `/` (`History`), and the user's navigations are `Navigate(h, href)` and `GoBackOn(h)`, or the acts `NavigateTo{Href}` and `GoBack`. The first navigation of a fresh session replaces the host's `/` rather than pushing, as a tab opened on an address has nothing to go back to, so its Back depth is 0 (FK9b); each later one pushes. `OpenAt(h, href)` is the tab opened on `href` before the program hears anything (a script's start address, a program started at a place): the history's one entry becomes `href` and the session counts as opened, so the next navigation pushes; it sends no `URLChanged`, as the program starts there already (UG-B22). `ReplaceOn(h, href)` changes the address in place, as a redirect or `replaceState` does, and reports it with `URLChanged` as a push is reported (FK10-g1). The law `NavigationFollowsModel` (in `TestUIHostModel`) checks drawn navigations, replacements and backs against a model stack whose first navigation replaces, and the shown place against the last change. *Decided (corpus wave H7, 2026-11-13):* a router's state lives in the program's model, reached by a signal, as Elm's `onUrlChange` and Lustre's `modem` do, not in a router of the library's. Reverse by a library router over `Location`. **Escaping and a typed query** (FK8a, FK10a): `Escape(s)` writes `s` as one URL component, a path segment or a query key or value, with every byte but a letter, a digit and `- . _ ~` as upper-case `%XX` (RFC 3986 2.1, 2.3), so no `/`, `?`, `#`, `&`, `=`, `+` or `%` of `s` ends the component; `Unescape` reads `%XX` in either case, and a `%` not followed by two hex digits stays as it is, so a hand-written address never fails (`+` stays a `+`: "a space" is the query's rule). `Segments(u)` is the path's segments, each unescaped, with the empty ones (a leading, doubled or trailing `/`) skipped; `PathOf(segments)` writes them back. `QueryOf[T wire](u) T fails http.QueryRepeated | wired[T]` reads a record from the query by its derived codec and std http's rule (`http.DecodeQuery` over `http.ParseQuery`, HTTP 3: a field's key is its wire name, an unknown key is skipped, a missing field fails unless it is a `json.Optional`, a scalar given twice fails); `EncodeQuery(v)` and `WithQuery(u, v)` write it, `key=value` per field in declaration order, each escaped, a list's key once per element, an absent `json.Optional` not at all, and a field with no query form (a record, a union value, a null) fails `QueryShape{Key}`. A field whose values are names (a union of empty variants) goes on the wire as a string by a by-name pair (`ParseX`, `Wire`; types.md, Codecs 1). The laws `EscapeRoundTrips`, `SegmentsReadPathOf` and `QueryRoundTrips` (in `TestUIHostModel`) hold the round trips on drawn texts of any byte, through `Href` and `ParseURL`. *Decided (corpus wave UC, 2026-11-13):* the query codec is std http's decoder and an encoder over the codec's events (`codec.Events`), so a URL's query and a request's query read by one rule; a parameter the program cannot read fails, and a program that wants a per-field default writes the field `json.Optional`. Reverse by a decoder of ui's own with a default per field.

*Prior art:* Elm's `Browser.application` gives `onUrlRequest` and `onUrlChange` messages and a `Nav.Key` for `pushUrl`, `replaceUrl` and `back`, as `URLChanged` and `Location` do here; Gleam's Lustre `modem` sends route changes as messages; Phoenix LiveView's `push_patch` and `handle_params` keep the route in the socket's assigns; ReScript React's `RescriptReactRouter.push` and `useUrl` read the browser's history; the web's `history.pushState` fires no `popstate`, as a `Push` sends no `URLChanged`.

18. **Same.** `Same(f, key) { … }` is a `Box` whose body runs only when its key changed (UC-4): when the last pass wrote this `Same` (its identity, as any node's) with an equal `Key`, the pass keeps the subtree the body wrote then, its nodes with their cells and loads, and makes again the `OnKey`, `OnURL` and `Animate` functions the body made, in their place; nothing under it runs, is compared at `Commit`, or is stored, so a pass costs what changed. The body must be a function of its key, of the cells it reads and of the `Fieldset`s around it: a cell write under the `Same` (a text in a `Field`, a press of a `Toggle` or a `Submit`, a `Scrolled` list), in it or in a `Same` inside it, runs it at the next pass, and so does a change of the disabled state around it, or a pass in which its node, or a node above it, comes back from being parked. `Skips(f)` is the number of `Same`s the last pass kept. The law `SameEqualsRecompute` (in `TestUITreeModel`) runs drawn programs with `Same`s, keyed by the state their bodies read, beside the same programs with every `Same` run each pass, and checks equal patches, trees and signals, and that a repeated pass keeps every outermost `Same`; `IncrementalEqualsFresh` checks the same programs against the model. A text event now writes only a `Field`'s draft: a `Type` on a `Toggle` or a `Submit` wrote a text into their cell (found by the law). *Decided (corpus wave UC-4, 2026-11-13):* the key is a `Key` the program gives, as Elm's `Html.Lazy` takes its arguments and React's `memo` its props, not an equality the compiler checks on what the body captures (P-ui's later form); the body keeps its contract in the program's hands. A cell write marks every `Same` around the cell's node, a chain of node references, so a `Same` elsewhere still skips. Reverse by a compiler-checked key.

*Prior art:* Elm's `Html.Lazy` skips a view function when its arguments are the same references; Gleam's Lustre builds and diffs the whole view tree on each update, as this composer did before `Same`; React's `memo` compares props; Jetpack Compose skips a restartable group whose parameters are stable and equal, over a slot table that keeps its nodes; Flutter reuses an element whose widget is `const` or identical; SwiftUI compares an `Equatable` view; Phoenix LiveView sends only the dynamics whose assigns changed, which its compiler tracks per template expression; ReScript uses React's `memo`.

19. **A view's row.** A view, a container's block and an `Each` or `Rows` row may need capabilities as any function does: the view type of `Render`, `Start`, `Run` and `NewTestHost` is `func(f mut Frame[E], s S) needs R`, each of them has `needs R`, and every container takes a block `func() fails F needs R`, so `R` is inferred (empty for a view that needs nothing). The program lists the services in its view's `needs` and binds them with a `with` around the host's entry in `cmd/<name>/main.ergo`, not as view parameters (Q16). `Start` holds the providers in force at its call (`InForce[R]()`, rows.md, Context values) and runs every pass of the session with them, so `Step` and the host loop need nothing. `Load[E, R](f, key, fetch)` takes a `fetch` that needs `R` and has `needs R`: it holds the providers in force at its call, the entry's or those of a `with` the view wrote around the subtree, and the fetch runs with them on the session's task after the pass, not with the task's. A load its node already holds with its key starts no fetch, so it holds no providers either: a kept load costs no allocation (measured on a 200-row view with a load per row: 3,624 allocations per pass before, 3,626 after, with and without a need). `Await(a)` reads an `Async[T, F]` as `T fails F | Pending`: the value when `Ready`, `Pending` while `Loading`, the failure when `Failed`, the exhaustive match written once, so a view writes `let u = try ui.Await(s.User)` under a boundary. The laws: `AwaitIsTheMatch` (`TestUILoadModel`) checks `Await` against the match on drawn values; `LoadUsesTheBoundService` (`TestUIHostModel`) draws views of one to three loads under their own components, each needing a drawn subset of three capabilities, with a drawn pair of providers each, half in an inner `with` of the other provider, and checks that every fetch's result came from the provider in force at its `Load`. `TestUIViewNeeds` holds the three diagnostics: a need the view's row does not list (S102), an entry with no `with` around it (S102), and a needing view given to a host entry whose view type has no `needs` (S108). *Decided (corpus wave H8b, 2026-11-13):* `Session` takes no row parameter: `Start` holds the providers, so the session's view needs nothing and `Step`, the test host's acts and every host loop keep their signatures. Reverse by a `Session[S, E, D, F, R]` whose `Step` has `needs R`.

*Prior art:* Elm's `Browser` programs take no services (a `Cmd` carries the effect, and the runtime runs it), so its view cannot reach one; Lustre's effects are the same. Effect's `Effect.provide` on a layer at the program's edge and `Effect.context<R>()` are what the `with` at the entry and `InForce` are here; React's context reaches a component through a provider above it, as an inner `with` reaches a subtree's loads. Phoenix LiveView reads services in `mount` and `handle_*` callbacks, which run in the LiveView's process. ReScript uses React's context.

20. **Crashes** (H6a). An app is a machine under `StartApp(m, capacity, view, crashes, post)`, `RunApp(h, m, capacity, view, crashes)` or `NewAppTestHost(m, capacity, view, crashes, clock, room)`: the session starts `m`'s actor on the providers in force and can start a fresh one. When that actor ends with a defect (of its step, its timer or its work) or with its step's typed failure, the app has crashed (`Crash[S, E, F]{Cause, State, Signal}`, FK13-g1: `Cause` is a `CrashCause[F] = DefectCrash{Reason} | FailureCrash[F]{Failure}`, a defect's `Reason` being `actor.Defected`'s, the runtime report's text; `State` is the last state the actor committed, the one the crashing step began from; `Signal` is `Some` of the session's signal when the actor ended once the session had sent it that signal, most likely its step's crash on it but possibly a timer's or a work's defect in the same moment, which the session cannot tell apart without a store per step, so `PlainCrash` says `signal: the last one the window sent (or a timer or work around it)`; `None` is a crash with no signal of the session's in flight, a timer's or a work's, shown as `signal: none (timer or work)` (law `PlainCrashSaysWhatItKnows`); `Crashes[S, E, F]`): the session calls `crashes.Report` once and shows `crashes.View(f, c)` from then on, not a view of the dead state; a signal of the crash view is a reload, which starts a fresh actor from `m`'s initial state, and the view's own state starts from its initial values (the crash view replaced its cells). `PlainCrashes()` is a line of text and no report, with no reload. A crash after a signal is seen at that signal (`actor.Can`, false only for an ended actor); one the loop did not cause (the work's, a timer's) reaches it as `Ended{Run}`, which the session's watcher posts when `a.LatestChanges()` ends, and an `Ended` of an actor a reload replaced, or of the crash already shown, changes nothing. A Done or a stop is an end, not a crash: the loop ends as `Run`'s does (`Stopped`). When the host closes on a crash, `RunApp` ends as the actor did, as its `Stop` does: it fails with the step's failure, or raises the defect. A declared failure of a view is never a crash (a host entry's view has an empty row, item 15). The test host's `CrashOf(h)` is the crash it shows. The laws: `CrashShowsTheCrashView` (drawn taps of adds, defects, typed failures and a check box against a model of the screen, the crash with its cause, reason, state and signal, and the number of reports) and `ReloadIsInitial` (after drawn taps, a crash and a reload, the screen is a fresh host's first screen) on the test host; `SessionCrashFollowsTheEnd` on a session as `RunApp` drives it (a work's defect reaches the loop as `Ended` with no signal, a step's carries its signal, each with its reason and state, and a stale `Ended` changes nothing); the loop model `models/uiloop` proves `NoFrameOfADeadActor` and `CrashShownOnce` with the mutants `DeadFrame` and `ReportEach`. *Decided (corpus wave H6a, 2026-11-13):* an app's entry takes the machine, not an actor, so a reload can start a fresh one; the crash view's signals all reload, since the crash view has no state of its own; the crash's report runs on the loop, before the crash view's first pass.
